\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}h^{kl}\left(\partial_{i}(g_{s})_{jl}+\partial_{j}(g_{s})_{il}-\partial_{l}(g_{s})_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}h^{kl}\left(\partial_{i}h_{jl}+\partial_{j}h_{il}-\partial_{l}h_{ij}\right)+h^{kj}h_{ji}g^{il}\left(\partial_{i}g_{jl}+\partial_{j}g_{il}-\partial_{l}g_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}h^{kl}\left(\partial_{i}h_{jl}+\partial_{j}h_{il}-\partial_{l}h_{ij}\right).

(4.5)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert C^{k}_{ij}\right\rvert\leq C[h]_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert D^{k}_{ij}\right\rvert\leq C[h]_{0}[h]_{1}.

(4.6)

Thus, by (
4
), (
4
) and (
4.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|(\mathrm{Ric}_{ij})_{s}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\partial_{l}C^{l}_{ij}-\partial_{i}C^{l}_{lj}+C^{a}_{ij}\Gamma^{l}_{la}+\Gamma^{a}_{ij}C^{l}_{la}-C^{a}_{lj}\Gamma^{l}_{ia}-\Gamma^{a}_{lj}C^{l}_{ia}\right|\leq C[h]_{2}

(4.7)

and

\displaystyle\left|(\mathrm{Ric}_{ij})_{ss}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\partial_{l}D^{l}_{ij}-\partial_{i}D^{l}_{lj}+D^{a}_{ij}\Gamma^{l}_{la}+\Gamma^{a}_{ij}D^{l}_{la}+2C^{a}_{ij}C^{l}_{la}-D^{a}_{lj}\Gamma^{l}_{ia}-\Gamma^{a}_{lj}D^{l}_{ia}-2C^{a}_{lj}C^{l}_{ia}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left([h]_{1}^{2}+[h]_{0}[h]_{2}\right).

(4.8)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left|(f_{ij})_{s}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\partial_{i}\partial_{j}f_{s}-C^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f-\Gamma^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f_{s}\right|\leq C\left([\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{1}\right),

(4.9)

and

\displaystyle\left|(f_{ij})_{ss}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\partial_{i}\partial_{j}f_{ss}-D^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f-C^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f_{s}-C^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f_{s}-\Gamma^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f_{ss}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|D^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f+2C^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}f_{s}\right|\leq C\left((1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}[h]_{1}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{0}\right).

(4.10)

Here, we have used the fact that
|\partial_{k}\bar{f}|\leq C|\vec{x}|
. Note that

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}(s)=\frac{g_{s}}{2}-\mathrm{Ric}_{s}-(\nabla^{2}f)_{s},\quad\Phi^{\prime\prime}(s)=\frac{g_{ss}}{2}-\mathrm{Ric}_{ss}-(\nabla^{2}f)_{ss}.

By (
4.7
), (
4
), (
4.9
) and (
4
), it follows that

\displaystyle|\Phi^{\prime}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{1}\right),

\displaystyle|\Phi^{\prime\prime}(s)|

\displaystyle\leq C\left([h]_{1}^{2}+[h]_{0}[h]_{2}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}[h]_{1}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{0}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.8
.

There exists a constant
C=C(n)>0
such that the following integral estimates hold for any
s\in[0,1]
:

(i)

\rVert\Phi^{\prime\prime}*h\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)
,

(ii)

\rVert\Phi^{\prime}*h*h\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)
,

(iii)

\rVert\Phi*h*h\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.7
, we have

\displaystyle\rVert\Phi^{\prime\prime}*h\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{1}^{2}[h]_{0}+[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{2}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{1}+[h]_{0}[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{0}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

(4.11)

Recall that
h=ug_{S^{m}}+\zeta
,
\chi=mu/2+q
and
\delta=\|h\|_{C^{2}}+\|\chi\|_{C^{2}}
. By Corollary
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle\rVert\zeta\rVert_{W^{2,2}}+\rVert\nabla q\rVert_{W^{1,2}}\leq C\mathfrak{X}.

(4.12)

We first estimate

\displaystyle[h]_{1}^{2}[h]_{0}\leq

\displaystyle C([u]_{1}^{2}+[\zeta]_{1}^{2})[h]_{0}\leq C\delta[\zeta]_{1}^{2}+C[u]_{1}^{2}[h]_{0}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta[\zeta]_{1}^{2}+C[u]_{1}^{2}([u]_{0}+[\zeta]_{0})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta[\zeta]_{1}^{2}+C\alpha^{3}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{6}+C\alpha^{2}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{4}[\zeta]_{0},

where we used Lemma
4.1
. From (
4.12
) and Hölder’s inequality, it is clear that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{1}^{2}[h]_{0}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.13)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\delta[\zeta]_{0}^{2}+C[u]_{0}^{2}[h]_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta[\zeta]_{0}^{2}+C[u]_{0}^{2}[u]_{2}+C[u]_{0}^{2}[\zeta]_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta[\zeta]_{0}^{2}+C\alpha^{3}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{6}+C\alpha^{2}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{4}[\zeta]_{2}

and hence

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.14)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C\delta(1+|\vec{x}|)[\zeta]_{0}^{2}+C(1+|\vec{x}|)[u]_{0}^{2}[h]_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta(1+|\vec{x}|)[\zeta]_{0}^{2}+C(1+|\vec{x}|)[u]_{0}^{2}[u]_{1}+C(1+|\vec{x}|)[u]_{0}^{2}[\zeta]_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta(1+|\vec{x}|)[\zeta]_{0}^{2}+C(1+|\vec{x}|)^{7}\alpha^{3}+C(1+|\vec{x}|)^{5}\alpha^{2}[\zeta]_{1}.

Since by Lemma
4.2
,

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)[\zeta]_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}\bar{f}[\zeta]_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}[\zeta]_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s},

we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{1}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.15)

In addition, we compute

\displaystyle[h]_{0}[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{0}\leq

\displaystyle[h]^{2}_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{0}\leq C([u]_{1}^{2}+[\zeta]_{1}^{2})[\nabla\chi]_{0}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C[u]_{1}^{2}[\nabla\chi]_{0}+C\delta[\zeta]_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C[u]_{1}^{2}([\nabla u]_{0}+[\nabla q]_{0})+C\delta[\zeta]_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\alpha^{3}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{5}+C\alpha^{2}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{4}[\nabla q]_{0}+C\delta[\zeta]_{1}^{2},

which implies

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{0}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.16)

Plugging all these estimates (
4.13
), (
4.14
), (
4.15
) and (
4.16
) into (
4.11
), we get

\displaystyle\rVert\Phi^{\prime\prime}*h\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

For
\rVert\Phi^{\prime}(s)*h*h\rVert_{L^{1}}
, it follows from Lemma
4.7
that

\displaystyle\rVert\Phi^{\prime}(s)*h*h\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{2}+[h]_{0}^{2}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{1}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

(4.17)

Notice that

\displaystyle[h]_{0}^{2}[\nabla\chi]_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C([u]_{0}^{2}+[\zeta]_{0}^{2})[\nabla\chi]_{1}\leq C[u]_{0}^{2}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+C\delta[\zeta]_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C[u]_{0}^{2}([\nabla u]_{1}+[\nabla q]_{1})+C\delta[\zeta]_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\alpha^{3}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{5}+C\alpha^{2}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{4}[\nabla q]_{1}+C\delta[\zeta]_{0}^{2},

which implies

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}^{2}[\nabla\chi]_{1}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

Combining this with (
4.14
), (
4.15
) and (
4.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\rVert\Phi^{\prime}(s)*h*h\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

Since
\Phi(0)=0
, we have
|\Phi(s)|\leq C\left([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{1}\right)
by integration, which implies

\displaystyle\rVert\Phi*h*h\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{2}+[h]_{0}^{2}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}^{2}[h]_{1}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

∎

Next, we consider the derivatives of
w(s)=2\Delta f-|\nabla f|^{2}+\mathrm{R}+f-n
with respect to
s
. The following variational formula is well-known to experts.

Lemma 4.9
.

We have

\displaystyle w^{\prime}(s)=-2\left(\Delta_{f}+\frac{1}{2}\right)Q+\langle\Phi,h\rangle+\mathrm{div}_{f}\mathrm{div}_{f}h.

(4.18)

Proof.

From direct calculations, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle-\langle\mathrm{Ric},h\rangle+\mathrm{div}\,\mathrm{div}\,h-\Delta(\mathrm{Tr}\,h),

\displaystyle\left(|\nabla f|^{2}\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle 2\langle\nabla f,\nabla\chi\rangle-h(\nabla f,\nabla f),

\displaystyle\left(\Delta f\right)^{\prime}=

\displaystyle-\langle\mathrm{div}\,h,\nabla f\rangle-\langle h,\nabla^{2}f\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle\nabla\mathrm{Tr}\,h,\nabla f\rangle+\Delta\chi,

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the inner product induced by
g(s)
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle w^{\prime}=-2\left(\Delta_{f}+\frac{1}{2}\right)Q+\langle\Phi,h\rangle+\mathrm{div}\,\mathrm{div}\,h-2\langle\mathrm{div}\,h,\nabla f\rangle-\langle h,\nabla^{2}f\rangle+h(\nabla f,\nabla f).

Since

\displaystyle\mathrm{div}_{f}\,\mathrm{div}_{f}h=\mathrm{div}\,\mathrm{div}\,h-2\langle\mathrm{div}\,h,\nabla f\rangle-\langle h,\nabla^{2}f\rangle+h(\nabla f,\nabla f),

we obtain (
4.18
).
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

The following pointwise bounds hold for
C=C(n)
and any
s\in[0,1]
:

(i)

|w^{\prime}(s)|\leq C\left([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+[Q]_{0}+[\Phi]_{0}[h]_{0}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}\right)
,

(ii)

|w^{\prime\prime}(s)|\leq C\left([h]_{2}^{2}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0}^{2}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{0}[h]_{1}+[h]_{0}[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}^{2}\right)
.

Proof.

(i): This follows from (
4.18
) directly.

(ii): We calculate the second derivative of
w
by

\displaystyle w^{\prime\prime}(s)=-2(\Delta_{f})^{\prime}Q-2\left(\Delta_{f}+\frac{1}{2}\right)Q^{\prime}+\langle\Phi^{\prime},h\rangle-2\Phi_{ij}h_{jl}h^{il}+\big(\mathrm{div}_{f}\mathrm{div}_{f}\big)^{\prime}h.

Note that
Q^{\prime}=-|h|_{g(s)}^{2}/2
and by Lemma
4.7
,
|\Phi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C\left([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{1}\right)
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left|-2\left(\Delta_{f}+\frac{1}{2}\right)Q^{\prime}+\langle\Phi^{\prime},h\rangle-2\Phi_{ij}h_{jl}h^{il}\right|\leq C\left([h]_{2}^{2}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}[h]_{1}\right).

(4.19)

Under local coordinates, we have

\displaystyle(\mathrm{div}_{f}h)_{i}=g^{jk}\left(\partial_{k}h_{ij}-\Gamma_{ki}^{l}h_{lj}-\Gamma_{kj}^{l}h_{il}\right)-g^{jk}h_{ij}\partial_{k}f,

then it follows that

\displaystyle(\mathrm{div}_{f}h)^{\prime}_{i}=-h^{jk}\left(\partial_{k}h_{ij}-\Gamma_{ki}^{l}h_{lj}-\Gamma_{kj}^{l}h_{il}\right)-g^{jk}(C_{ki}^{l}h_{lj}+C_{kj}^{l}h_{il})+h^{jk}h_{ij}\partial_{k}f-g^{jk}h_{ij}\partial_{k}\chi,

where
C_{ij}^{k}=(\Gamma_{ij}^{k})_{s}
, as in the proof of Lemma
4.7
. By the expression (
4
), this implies

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{div}_{f}(\mathrm{div}_{f}h)^{\prime}\right|\leq C\left([h]_{2}^{2}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{0}[h]_{1}+[h]_{0}[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}^{2}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|(\mathrm{div}_{f}\mathrm{div}_{f}h)^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathrm{div}_{f}(\mathrm{div}_{f}h)^{\prime}+(\mathrm{div}_{f})^{\prime}(\mathrm{div}_{f}h)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left([h]_{2}^{2}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{0}[h]_{1}+[h]_{0}[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}^{2}\right).

(4.20)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle(\Delta_{f})^{\prime}Q=-h^{ij}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}Q+h^{ij}\Gamma^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}Q-g^{ij}C^{k}_{ij}\partial_{k}Q+h^{ij}\partial_{i}f\partial_{j}Q-g^{ij}\partial_{i}\chi\partial_{j}Q

and hence

\displaystyle\left|(\Delta_{f})^{\prime}Q\right|\leq C\left([h]_{1}[\nabla Q]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0}[\nabla Q]_{0}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}[\nabla Q]_{0}\right).

(4.21)

Combining (
4.19
), (
4
), (
4.21
) and noting that
[\nabla Q]_{0}\leq[h]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0}
and
[\nabla Q]_{1}\leq[h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|w^{\prime\prime}(s)\right|\leq C\left([h]_{2}^{2}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0}^{2}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{0}[h]_{1}+[h]_{0}[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}^{2}\right).

This completes the proof of (ii).
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

There exists a constant
C=C(n)
such that

\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(s)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{4}+\delta^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+2\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}.

Proof.

Set
\eta(s):=\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(s)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}
. Since
|Q|\leq C\delta
and
Q^{\prime}=-|h|^{2}/2
, we calculate

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}(s)=\int_{\bar{M}}2Q(s)Q^{\prime}(s)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{3}(s)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq-\int_{\bar{M}}Q(s)|h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+C\delta\eta(s).

(4.22)

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}|Q(s)||h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq\left(\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right)^{1/2}\eta^{1/2}(s).

(4.23)

Note that

\displaystyle|h|^{4}\leq C(u^{4}+|\zeta|^{4})\leq Cu^{4}+C|\zeta|^{2}(|h|^{2}+u^{2})\leq Cu^{4}+C\delta^{2}|\zeta|^{2}+C\alpha^{2}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{4}|\zeta|^{2},

which, by using Lemma
4.2
and Corollary
4.5
, implies

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{4}+\delta^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

Thus, it follows from (
4.23
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}|Q(s)||h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}+\delta\mathfrak{X}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}\right)\eta^{1/2}(s).

(4.24)

Combining (
4.22
) and (
4.24
), we obtain

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}(s)\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}+\delta\mathfrak{X}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}\right)\eta^{1/2}(s)+C\delta\eta.

If we set
\psi(s)=(e^{-C\delta s}\eta)^{1/2}
, then

\psi^{\prime}\leq\frac{C}{2}e^{-\frac{C\delta s}{2}}\left(\alpha^{2}+\delta\mathfrak{X}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}\right).

Therefore, by integration, we obtain

\psi(s)\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}+\delta\mathfrak{X}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}\right)+\psi(0)=C\left(\alpha^{2}+\delta\mathfrak{X}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}\right)+\eta^{1/2}(0).

Since
\delta
is sufficiently small, we have

\eta(s)\leq C\left(\alpha^{4}+\delta^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+2\eta(0).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.12
.

There exists a constant
C=C(n)
such that

\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(s)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|^{2}\right).

Proof.

For the function
V(s)=\int_{\bar{M}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}
, a direct calculation shows that

V^{\prime}=\int_{\bar{M}}Q\,\mathrm{d}V_{s},\quad V^{\prime\prime}=\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{\prime}+Q^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s},\quad V^{\prime\prime\prime}=\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{\prime\prime}+3Q^{\prime}Q+Q^{3}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

Thus,

V^{\prime}(0)=\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0},\quad V^{\prime\prime}(0)=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}+\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}.

Using the fact that
Q^{\prime}=-|h|^{2}/2
, we have
Q^{\prime\prime}=h*h*h
, and hence for any
s\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{3}+|h|^{2}|Q|+\delta Q^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

(4.25)

Notice that

\displaystyle|h|^{3}\leq C(|u|^{3}+|\zeta|^{3})\leq C|u|^{3}+C|\zeta|^{2}(|h|+|u|)\leq C|u|^{3}+C\delta|\zeta|^{2}+C\alpha(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}|\zeta|^{2},

(4.26)

which, by using Lemma
4.2
and Corollary
4.5
, implies

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{3}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.27)

In addition, it follows from (
4.24
) and Lemma
4.11
that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{2}|Q(s)|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{4}+\delta^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+C\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}.

(4.28)

Combining (
4.25
), (
4.27
), (
4.28
) and Lemma
4.11
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\prime\prime\prime}\right\rvert\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+C\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}.

(4.29)

By the Taylor expansion of
V
, we have

\left|V(1)-V(0)-V^{\prime}(0)-\frac{1}{2}V^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leq\frac{1}{6}\sup_{s\in[0,1]}|V^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)|.

By using (
4.29
), this implies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\alpha^{3}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+C\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|.

(4.30)

Since
\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\leq C(\alpha^{2}+\mathfrak{X}^{2})
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+C\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|.

(4.31)

On the other hand, by the Poincaré inequality on the cylinder, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}-\left(\int Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right)^{2}\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}\left|\nabla Q(0)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2},

(4.32)

where the last inequality follows from

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}|\nabla Q(0)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\leq C\left(\rVert\zeta\rVert_{W^{1,2}}^{2}+\rVert\nabla q\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2}.

(4.33)

Combining (
4.31
) with (
4.32
), we get

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+C\left(\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right)^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|.

Since
\left|\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\delta
, we conclude that if
\delta\ll 1
,

\left|\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right)+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|.

Therefore, by Corollary
4.5
and (
4.32
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left(\alpha^{2}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right)^{2}\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2}+C\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|^{2}.

(4.34)

Then Proposition
4.12
follows from the combination of Lemma
4.11
and (
4.34
).
∎

Lemma 4.13
.

There exists a constant
C=C(n)
such that for any
s\in[0,1]
,

\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}Q^{2}(s)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\delta\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+|V(1)-V(0)|^{2}\right).

Proof.

We set
\eta(s)=\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}Q^{2}(s)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}
. By Lemma
4.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\eta(0)=\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}Q(0)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\leq C\left(\|Q(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla Q(0)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\right)\leq C\left(\mathfrak{X}^{2}+|V(1)-V(0)|^{2}\right),

(4.35)

where we used Proposition
4.12
and (
4.33
). By a direct calculation, as in the proof of Lemma
4.11
, we have

\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}(s)\leq\left(\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}|h|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right)^{1/2}\eta^{1/2}(s)+C\delta\eta(s).

(4.36)

From (
4.26
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}|h|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\delta\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}\left(|u|^{3}+\delta|\zeta|^{2}+\alpha(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}|\zeta|^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s},

which, by using Lemma
4.2
and Corollary
4.5
, implies

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}|h|^{4}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\delta\left(\alpha^{3}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.37)

Combining (
4.35
), (
4.36
), and (
4.37
), we deduce, by the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.11
, that

\displaystyle\eta(s)\leq C\left(\delta\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+|V(1)-V(0)|^{2}\right).

∎

Lemma 4.14
.

There exists a constant
C=C(n)
such that for any
s\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\rVert\Phi^{\prime}*h*Q\rVert_{L^{1}}+\rVert\Phi*h*Q\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
4.7
, we have
|\Phi(s)|+|\Phi^{\prime}(s)|\leq C\left([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{1}\right)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\rVert\Phi*h*Q\rVert_{L^{1}}+\rVert\Phi^{\prime}*h*Q\rVert_{L^{1}}\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}\left([h]_{2}^{2}+[h]_{0}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{1}^{2}\right)|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

Now we estimate these three terms separately. For the first term, by Lemma
4.1
and Proposition
4.12
,

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{2}^{2}|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{2}\int_{\bar{M}}\left(1+|\vec{x}|^{4}\right)|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+C\int_{\bar{M}}[\zeta]_{2}^{2}|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{2}\rVert Q\rVert_{L^{2}}+C\delta\|\zeta\|^{2}_{W^{2,2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.38)

Similarly, for the second term, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}[\nabla\chi]_{1}|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq

\displaystyle C\alpha^{2}\int_{\bar{M}}\left(1+|\vec{x}|^{4}\right)|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+C\int_{\bar{M}}\left([\zeta]_{0}^{2}+[\nabla q]_{1}^{2}\right)|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.39)

For the third term, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and Lemma
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}\left(1+|\vec{x}|\right)[h]_{1}^{2}|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{2}\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{5}|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+C\int_{\bar{M}}\left(1+|\vec{x}|\right)[\zeta]_{1}^{2}|Q|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{2}\rVert Q\rVert_{L^{2}}+C\delta\int_{\bar{M}}\left(1+|\vec{x}|\right)[\zeta]_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha^{2}\rVert Q\rVert_{L^{2}}+C\delta\|\zeta\|_{W^{2,2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

(4.40)

Combining (
4
), (
4
) and (
4
), we complete the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.15
.

There exists a constant
C=C(n)
such that

\left|W^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)\right|\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|+\delta\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|^{2}\right).

Proof.

Note that
(\mathrm{d}V_{s})^{\prime}=Q\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}
, we thus have
W^{\prime}(s)=\int w^{\prime}+wQ\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}
and by Lemma
4.9
, we can rewrite it as

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(s)=\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\Phi(s),h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+\int_{\bar{M}}Q(w-1)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

(4.41)

Then we have

\displaystyle W^{\prime\prime}(s)=

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\Phi^{\prime},h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+\int_{\bar{M}}-2\Phi_{ij}h_{jl}h^{il}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\Phi,h\rangle Q\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\quad(=:\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}+\mathrm{III})

\displaystyle+\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{\prime}(w-1)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+\int_{\bar{M}}Qw^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}+\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(w-1)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\quad(=:\mathrm{IV}+\mathrm{V}+\mathrm{VI}).

(4.42)

We now estimate the third derivative term by term using Corollary
4.8
, Lemma
4.10
, and Lemma
4.14
.
First, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{I}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\Phi^{\prime\prime},h\rangle+\Phi^{\prime}*h*h+\langle\Phi^{\prime},h\rangle Q\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\rVert\Phi^{\prime\prime}*h\rVert_{L^{1}}+\rVert\Phi^{\prime}*h*h\rVert_{L^{1}}+\rVert\langle\Phi^{\prime},h\rangle Q\rVert_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right),

where in the last inequality, we have used Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.14
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{II}^{\prime}\right\rvert=

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\bar{M}}\Phi^{\prime}*h*h+\Phi*h*h*h+\Phi^{\prime}*h*hQ\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right),

where in the last inequality, we have used Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.14
.

In the same way, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{III}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\Phi^{\prime},h\rangle Q+\Phi*h*hQ-\frac{1}{2}\langle\Phi,h\rangle|h|^{2}+\langle\Phi,h\rangle Q^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right).

Note that
Q^{\prime}=-|h|^{2}/2
and
Q^{\prime\prime}=h*h*h
. It follows from Lemma
4.10
that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{IV}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{\prime\prime}(w-1)+Q^{\prime}w^{\prime}+Q^{\prime}Q(w-1)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}^{2}([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+[Q]_{0}+[\Phi]_{0}[h]_{0}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0})\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

By similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.14
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}^{2}\left([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+[Q]_{0}+[\Phi]_{0}[h]_{0}\right)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right),

and

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)[h]_{0}^{2}([h]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}^{3}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}\right).

Thus, it follows that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{IV}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right).

For the fifth term, by Lemma
4.10
and a similar argument as above, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{V}^{\prime}\right\rvert=

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{\prime}w^{\prime}+Qw^{\prime\prime}+Q^{2}w^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\bar{M}}[h]_{0}^{2}\left([h]_{2}+[\nabla\chi]_{1}+[Q]_{0}+[\Phi]_{0}[h]_{0}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}\right)

\displaystyle+[Q]_{0}\left([h]_{2}^{2}+[h]_{1}[\nabla\chi]_{1}+[\nabla\chi]_{0}^{2}+(1+|\vec{x}|)([h]_{0}[h]_{1}+[h]_{0}[\nabla\chi]_{0})+(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})[h]_{0}^{2}\right)+Q^{2}|w^{\prime}|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

Using the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.14
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{V}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right)+C\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}|w^{\prime}|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}.

The last integral can be estimated by Lemmas
4.10
and (
4.13
) as

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}|w^{\prime}|\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\delta\int_{\bar{M}}(1+|\vec{x}|)^{2}Q^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\leq C\delta\left(\delta\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+|V(1)-V(0)|^{2}\right).

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{V}^{\prime}\right\rvert\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|+\delta\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|^{2}\right).

Finally, the last term can be similarly estimated as

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{VI}^{\prime}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\int_{\bar{M}}2QQ^{\prime}(w-1)+Q^{2}w^{\prime}+Q^{3}(w-1)\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\bar{M}}|Q||h|^{2}+|w^{\prime}|Q^{2}+\delta Q^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{s}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\delta^{2}\alpha^{3}+\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}+\delta\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\alpha^{2}\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|+\delta\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|^{2}\right).

This finishes the proof of Proposition
4.15
.
∎

Applying the Taylor expansion to
W
, we have

\left|W(1)-W(0)-W^{\prime}(0)-\frac{1}{2}W^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leq\frac{1}{6}\sup_{s\in[0,1]}|W^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)|.

(4.43)

It is clear from (
4.41
), (
4.42
) and Lemma
4.6
for
s=0
that

\displaystyle W^{\prime}(0)=

\displaystyle(W(0)-1)\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}

(4.44)

and

\displaystyle W^{\prime\prime}(0)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\mathcal{L}h+2\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}^{*}\,\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h+2\nabla^{2}Q(0),h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}+\int_{\bar{M}}2|\nabla Q(0)|^{2}-Q^{2}(0)+Q(0)\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}

\displaystyle+(W(0)-1)\int_{\bar{M}}(Q(0)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}|h|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}.

(4.45)

Next, we prove

Lemma 4.16
.

We can find a constant
C=C(n)
such that

\displaystyle\left|W^{\prime}(0)+\frac{1}{2}W^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right).

Proof.

By the Taylor expansion of
V
in (
4
) (note that here we can improve the estimate in (
4
) by (
4.34
)), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\bar{M}}|h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bar{M}}Q^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right).

Thus, it follows from (
4.44
) and (
4.45
) that

\displaystyle\left|W^{\prime}(0)+\frac{1}{2}W^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\mathcal{L}h+2\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}^{*}\,\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h+2\nabla^{2}Q(0),h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\left(2|\nabla Q(0)|^{2}-Q^{2}(0)+Q(0)\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\right)\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|

\displaystyle+C\left(\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right).

By Proposition
4.12
and (
4.33
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{M}}|Q(0)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\leq C\left(\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|^{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\bar{M}}|\nabla Q(0)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2}.

Using the integration by parts, which is justified since
h
is compactly supported, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}Q(0)\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|=\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\nabla Q(0),\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq\rVert\nabla Q(0)\rVert_{L^{2}}\rVert\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2},

where we used
\rVert\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rVert_{L^{2}}\leq\mathfrak{X}
from the definition of
\mathfrak{X}
.

Since
\mathcal{L}(ug_{S^{m}})=0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\mathcal{L}h,h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|=\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\mathcal{L}\zeta,\zeta\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\|\zeta\|_{W^{1,2}}^{2}\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2}.

Moreover, using integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}^{*}\,\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h,h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|=\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h,\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\nabla^{2}Q(0),h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|=\left|\int_{\bar{M}}\langle\nabla Q(0),\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rangle\,\mathrm{d}V_{0}\right|\leq C\mathfrak{X}^{2}.

Since
\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\ll 1
, we can combine all the above inequalities to conclude that

\left|W^{\prime}(0)+\frac{1}{2}W^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leq C\left(\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|V(1)-V(0)\right|\right).

∎

The main result of this section is the following estimate for
\mathcal{W}(g,f)
.

Theorem 4.17
.

For the weighted Riemannian manifold
(\bar{M},g,f)
such that
(h:=g-\bar{g},\chi:=f-\bar{f})\in C^{2}(S^{2}(\bar{M}))\times C^{2}(\bar{M})
is compactly supported and

\displaystyle\|h\|_{C^{2}}+\|\chi\|_{C^{2}}\leq\delta_{n},

for a small constant
\delta_{n}
depending only
n
, then we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{W}(g,f)-\Theta_{m}\right|\leq C\left(\rVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}^{3}+\rVert\mathbf{\Phi}(g,f)\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}+\rVert\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rVert_{W^{1,2}}^{2}+|\mathcal{B}(h,\chi)|^{2}+\left|\mathcal{V}(g,f)-1\right|\right),

where
ug_{S^{m}}
is the projection of
h
onto
\mathcal{K}_{0}g_{S^{m}}
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{W}(g,f)=W(1)
,
\mathcal{W}(\bar{g},\bar{f})=W(0)=\Theta_{m}
,
\mathcal{V}(g,f)=V(1)
and
\mathcal{V}(\bar{g},\bar{f})=1
, then the conclusion follows from the combination of Proposition
4.15
, Lemma
4.16
and (
4.43
), noting that
\alpha^{2}\mathfrak{X}\leq 2\alpha^{3}/3+\mathfrak{X}^{3}/3
.
∎

5
Radius functions on weighted Riemannian manifolds

In this section, we introduce several notions of radius functions that serve to characterize regions that are nearly cylindrical on a weighted Riemannian manifold. Our main result, Theorem
5.4
, provides an estimate for the deviation of the pointed
\mathcal{W}
-entropy from that of the standard cylinder, expressed in terms of a suitable curvature radius.

Definitions of radius functions

Throughout this subsection, the weighted Riemannian manifold
\left(M,g,f\right)
is always assumed to be normalized (see (
2.1
)) and satisfies, for some constant
C_{V}>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{M\setminus B(p,L)}1\,\mathrm{d}V_{f}\leq C_{V}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{15}},\quad\forall L>0,

(5.1)

where
p
is a fixed minimum point of
f
. As in Section
4
, we fix a weighted cylinder
\left(\bar{M},\bar{g},\bar{f}\right)
as in (
4.1
), set
\bar{b}=2\sqrt{\bar{f}}
, and fix a base point
\bar{p}\in\bar{M}
, which is a minimum point of
\bar{f}
.

Definition 5.1
.

For
\sigma\in(0,1/10)
and a weighted Riemannian manifold
(M,g,f)
with
\Phi=\mathbf{\Phi}(g,f)=\dfrac{g}{2}-\mathrm{Ric}(g)-\nabla^{2}f
, we define

(A)

(
\textbf{r}_{A}
-radius)

\textbf{r}_{A}
as the largest number
L
such that there exists a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{A}
from
\{\bar{b}\leq L\}\subset\bar{M}
onto a subset of
M
such that

\displaystyle\left[\bar{g}-\varphi_{A}^{*}g\right]_{5}+\left[\bar{f}-\varphi_{A}^{*}f\right]_{5}\leq e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{4}-\frac{L^{2}}{16}},

(B)

(
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}
-radius)

\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}
as the largest number
L
such that there exists a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{B}
from
\{\bar{b}\leq L\}\subset\bar{M}
onto a subset of
M
such that

\displaystyle\left[\bar{g}-\varphi_{B}^{*}g\right]_{0}+\left[\bar{f}-\varphi_{B}^{*}f\right]_{0}\leq e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{33}},

and

\int_{\{\bar{b}\leq L\}}\left|\varphi_{B}^{*}\Phi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}\leq e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4-\sigma}}.

(5.2)

Moreover, for all
k\in[1,10^{10}n\sigma^{-1}]
, the
C^{k}
-norms of
\bar{g}-\varphi_{B}^{*}g
and
\bar{f}-\varphi_{B}^{*}f
are bounded by
1
.

Here and for the remainder of the paper, all norms
[\cdot]_{l}
are taken with respect to
\bar{g}
, unless explicitly stated otherwise.

In the following, we will frequently use the interpolation inequalities from
[
Kry96
, Section 3.2]
and
[
CMI15
, Lemma B.1]
. Although originally stated for Euclidean space, these inequalities remain valid on Riemannian manifolds with bounded geometry. In particular, they apply to
(\bar{M},\bar{g})
. Let
a_{l,n}:=\frac{l}{l+n}
denote the constants introduced in
[
CMI15
, Lemma B.1]
. Unless otherwise specified, all Sobolev norms considered in this section are weighted norms with respect to
\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}
.

Comparison of radius functions I

The following proposition is similar to
[
LZ23
, Theorem 5.2]
:

Proposition 5.2
.

With assumption (
5.1
), there exists a constant
L_{1}=L_{1}(n,C_{V},\sigma)>0
such that if
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}\geq L_{1}
, then

\textbf{r}_{A}\geq\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}-2.

Proof.

For simplicity, we set
L=\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}
and let
\varphi_{B}
denote the diffeomorphism corresponding to
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}
.

Throughout the proof, we denote by
C
constants depending only on
n
, and by
C^{\prime}
constants depending only on
n,C_{V}
. All these constants can be different line by line.

Choose a cut-off function
\eta
that is supported in
\{\bar{b}<L-1/2\}
and is identically
1
on
\{\bar{b}\leq L-1\}
. By Definition
5.1
(B) and the multiplicative interpolation (see
[
Kry96
, Theorem 3.2.1]
) on each ball of size
1/4
, we have on
\{\bar{b}\leq L-1/4\}
,

\displaystyle\rVert\bar{g}-\varphi_{B}^{*}g\rVert_{C^{k,\frac{1}{2}}}+\rVert\bar{f}-\varphi_{B}^{*}f\rVert_{C^{k,\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{34}},

(5.3)

for any
k\in[1,10^{8}n\sigma^{-1}]
, provided that
L
is sufficiently large.

As in the proof of
[
LZ23
, Theorem 5.2]
(see
[
LZ23
, Equations (5.15), (5.16)]
), there exists a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{A}
, which is a modification of
\varphi_{B}
by some diffeomorphism, from
\{\bar{b}\leq L-1/2\}
to a subset of
M
such that for
h:=\eta\big(\varphi_{A}^{*}g-\bar{g}\big),\chi:=\eta\left(\varphi_{A}^{*}f-\bar{f}\right)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bar{b}\leq L-1/2}\left(\rVert h\rVert_{C^{k,\frac{1}{2}}}+\rVert\chi\rVert_{C^{k,\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\leq e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{34}}\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{\bar{b}\leq L-6}\rVert\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rVert_{C^{2,\frac{1}{2}}}+\left|\mathcal{B}(h,\chi)\right|\leq e^{-\frac{(L-6)^{2}}{8}-\frac{L^{2}}{34}},

(5.4)

for any
k\in[1,10^{6}n\sigma^{-1}]
, provided that
L
is sufficiently large.

In particular, by Lemma
4.7
(i), we see that for
k\in[1,10^{6}n\sigma^{-1}]
,

\rVert\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)\rVert_{C^{k-2}}\leq CLe^{-\frac{L^{2}}{34}}.

(5.5)

Now, we write as in (
4.2
):

\displaystyle h=ug_{S^{m}}+\zeta\quad\text{and}\quad\chi=\frac{m}{2}u+q

such that
ug_{S^{m}}
is the projection of
h
to
\mathcal{K}_{0}g_{S^{m}}
. Then, it follows from Proposition
4.4
that

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}:=\rVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rVert(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)\rVert_{L^{1}}+C_{\epsilon}\left(\rVert\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2-\epsilon}+|\mathcal{B}(h,\chi)|^{2-\epsilon}+\rVert\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rVert_{W^{1,2}}^{2-\epsilon}\right).

(5.6)

Since
\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)=\varphi_{A}^{*}\Phi
on
\{\bar{b}\leq L-1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}|\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}=\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}|\varphi_{A}^{*}\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}\leq C\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}|\varphi_{A}^{*}\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\varphi_{A}^{*}f}=C\int_{\varphi_{A}(\{\bar{b}<L-1\})}|\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{f},

where we used (
5.4
) for the inequality. Since
\varphi_{A}
is equal to
\varphi_{B}
up to an exponential error (see
[
LZ23
, Equations (5.9)]
), it follows from (
5.2
) and (
5.5
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\{\bar{b}<L\}}|\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\{\bar{b}<L\}}|\varphi_{B}^{*}\Phi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}+\int_{\{L-1\leq\bar{b}<L\}}|\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4-\sigma}}+L^{n+2}e^{-\frac{(L-1)^{2}}{4}-\frac{L^{2}}{17}}\right)\leq Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4-\sigma}}.

Moreover, by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\rVert(1+|\vec{x}|^{2})\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)\rVert_{L^{1}(\{\bar{b}<L\})}

\displaystyle\leq C\rVert\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)\rVert_{L^{2}(\{\bar{b}<L\})}\leq Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{2(4-\sigma)}}.

Using (
5.4
), (
5.6
) and choosing
\epsilon
to be small, we obtain

\alpha\leq Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.7)

Moreover, by Proposition
4.3
, it follows that

\displaystyle\rVert\zeta\rVert_{W^{2,2}}+\rVert\nabla q\rVert_{W^{1,2}}\leq C\left(\rVert\mathbf{\Phi}(\bar{g}+h,\bar{f}+\chi)\rVert_{L^{2}}+\rVert\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rVert_{W^{1,2}}+|\mathcal{B}(h,\chi)|+\alpha^{2}\right)\leq Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{2(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.8)

For any
x\in\{\bar{b}<L-1\}
, applying the interpolation (see
[
CMI15
, Lemma B.1]
) to the function
U:=([\zeta]_{2}^{2}+[\nabla q]_{1}^{2})e^{-\bar{f}}
on
B(x,1/2)
, we get that for
k=10^{6}n\sigma^{-1}-2
,

\displaystyle\rVert U\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x,1/4))}\leq

\displaystyle C\rVert U\rVert_{L^{1}(B(x,1/2))}+C\rVert U\rVert_{L^{1}(B(x,1/2))}^{a_{k,n}}\rVert\nabla^{k}U\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B(x,1/2))}^{1-a_{k,n}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4-\sigma}}+CL^{k}e^{-a_{k,n}\frac{L^{2}}{4-\sigma}-(1-a_{k,n})\frac{L^{2}}{17}}\leq Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4-0.9\sigma}}.

(5.9)

Here, in the second inequality, we have used (
5.3
) and (
5.8
). Note that in (
5
), the Sobolev norms are unweighted.

It follows from (
5.7
) and (
5
) that on
\{\bar{b}<L-1\}
,

[\zeta]_{2}+[\nabla q]_{1}\leq Ce^{\frac{\bar{f}}{2}-\frac{L^{2}}{8-1.8\sigma}},\quad[u]_{2}\leq C\bar{f}\alpha\leq C\bar{f}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.10)

To get the estimate for
q
itself, we use the normalization condition and the assumption (
5.1
). In fact, by (
5.1
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\{b>L-2\}}e^{-f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g}\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}},

thus, it follows from the normalization condition that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}e^{-\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}-\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}e^{-\varphi_{A}^{*}f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\varphi_{A}^{*}g}\right|\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.11)

Since
\left|\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}-\,\mathrm{d}V_{\varphi_{A}^{*}g}\right|\leq C[h]_{0}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}\leq C\left([\zeta]_{0}+[u]_{0}\right)\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}e^{-\varphi_{A}^{*}f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}-\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}e^{-\varphi_{A}^{*}f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\varphi_{A}^{*}g}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}e^{-\chi-\bar{f}}\,\left(\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}-\mathrm{d}V_{\varphi_{A}^{*}g}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\rVert\zeta\rVert_{L^{1}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq L-1\}\right)}+\rVert u\rVert_{L^{1}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq L-1\}\right)}\right)\leq Ce^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.12)

On the other hand, by definition,

\displaystyle\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}e^{-\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}-\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}e^{-\varphi_{A}^{*}f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}=\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}(e^{-\chi}-1)e^{-\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}},

we thus obtain from (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) that

\left|\int_{\{\bar{b}<L-1\}}(e^{-\chi}-1)e^{-\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}\right|\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.13)

By (
5.4
), we have

\left|\int_{\{L/2\leq\bar{b}<L-1\}}(e^{-\chi}-1)e^{-\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}\right|\leq CL^{n}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{34}-\frac{L^{2}}{16}}.

(5.14)

Combinining (
5.13
) and (
5.14
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\{\bar{b}<L/2\}}(e^{-\chi}-1)e^{-\bar{f}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}}\right|\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

Therefore, there exists a point
z_{0}\in\{\bar{b}<L/2\}
such that
|\chi(z_{0})|\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}
. By (
5.10
), this implies

\left|q(z_{0})\right|\leq|\chi(z_{0})|+C|u(z_{0})|\leq C^{\prime}L^{2}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.15)

Combining (
5.15
) with (
5.10
) again, it follows that on
\{\bar{b}<L-1\}
,

|q|\leq C\bar{b}e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{2}-\frac{L^{2}}{8-1.8\sigma}}+C^{\prime}L^{2}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

(5.16)

Indeed, (
5.16
) is obviously true on
\{L/2\leq\bar{b}<L-1\}
. On the other hand, if
z\in\{\bar{b}<L/2\}
, it follows from (
5.10
) and (
5.15
) that

|q(z)|\leq CLe^{\frac{L^{2}}{32}-\frac{L^{2}}{8-1.8\sigma}}+|q(z_{0})|\leq C^{\prime}L^{2}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}.

By (
5.10
) and (
5.16
), we can conclude that on
\{\bar{b}<L-1\}
,

[h]_{2}+[\chi]_{2}\leq C\bar{b}e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{2}-\frac{L^{2}}{8-1.8\sigma}}+C\bar{f}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}+C^{\prime}L^{2}e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4(4-\sigma)}}\leq C^{\prime}e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{4}-\frac{L^{2}}{16-3\sigma}},

(5.17)

where in the last inequality, we have used the following facts:

\displaystyle\bar{f}\leq Ce^{\frac{\bar{f}}{4}}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{b}e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{4}-\frac{L^{2}}{8-1.8\sigma}+\frac{L^{2}}{16-3\sigma}}\leq CLe^{\frac{(L-1)^{2}}{16}-\frac{L^{2}}{16}}\leq C.

Using (
5.3
), (
5.17
) and another interpolation at the scale of
L^{-1}
, we conclude that on
\{\bar{b}\leq L-2\}

[h]_{5}+[\chi]_{5}\leq e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{4}-\frac{(L-2)^{2}}{16}}.

(5.18)

In summary, the diffeomorphism
\varphi_{A}
together with the estimates in (
5.18
) ensures that the conditions in the definition of
\textbf{r}_{A}
are satisfied.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

From the proof of Proposition
5.2
, it is clear that the constant
15
in (
5.1
) can be replaced with any positive constant smaller than
16-5\sigma
.

The proof of Proposition
5.2
also yields the following result:

Theorem 5.4
.

There exist constants
L_{2}=L_{2}(n,Y,\sigma)>0
and
C=C(n,Y)>0
such that the following holds.
Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M,(g(t))_{t\in I}\}
be a closed Ricci flow with entropy bounded below by
-Y
. Assume
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}
and
[t_{0}-2r^{2},t_{0}]\subset I
. If the weighted Riemannian manifold
\left(M,r^{-2}g(t_{0}-r^{2}),f_{x_{0}^{*}}(t_{0}-r^{2})\right)
satisfies
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}\geq L_{2}
, then

\left|\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r^{2})-\Theta_{m}\right|\leq C\exp\left(-\frac{3\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{2}}{16}\right),

where
\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}
is the pointed
\mathcal{W}
-entropy at
x_{0}^{*}
; see Definition
2.7
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
t_{0}=0
,
r=1
and choose a small parameter
\epsilon\ll 1
to be determined later. Also, we set
L=\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}
,
g_{0}=g(-1)
and
f_{0}=f_{x_{0}^{*}}(-1)
for simplicity.

By Definition
5.1
, we can find a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{B}
from
\{\bar{b}\leq L\}
onto a subset of
M
such that

\displaystyle\left[\bar{g}-\varphi_{B}^{*}g_{0}\right]_{0}+\left[\bar{f}-\varphi_{B}^{*}f_{0}\right]_{0}\leq e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{33}},

and

\int_{\{\bar{b}<L\}}\left|\varphi_{B}^{*}\Phi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{f}}\leq e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4-\sigma}},

where
\Phi=g_{0}/2-\mathrm{Ric}\left(g_{0}\right)-\nabla^{2}f_{0}
. Furthermore, all
C^{k}
-norms of
\bar{g}-\varphi_{B}^{*}g_{0}
and
\bar{f}-\varphi_{B}^{*}f_{0}
are bounded by
1
for
k\in[1,10^{10}n\sigma^{-1}]
. By Theorem
2.13
(i), the assumption (
5.1
) holds for
C_{V}=C_{V}(n,Y)
. Now we choose
L_{2}=L_{1}(n,C_{V})
, where
L_{1}
is the same constant as in Proposition
5.2
so that it applies to the weighted Riemannian manifold
\left(M,g(-1),f(-1)\right)
.

Choose a cut-off function
\eta
on
\bar{M}
such that
\eta=0
outside
\{\bar{b}<L-1\}
and
\eta=1
on
\{\bar{b}<L-2\}
. Let
g_{1}=\bar{g}+\eta(\varphi_{B}^{*}g_{0}-\bar{g}),f_{1}=\bar{f}+\eta(\varphi_{B}^{*}f_{0}-\bar{f})
. By Proposition
3.1
and construction, we have

\left|\mathcal{W}(g_{0},f_{0})-\mathcal{W}(g_{1},f_{1})\right|\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{-\frac{(1-\epsilon)L^{2}}{4}}.

(5.19)

By the proof of Proposition
5.2
(see (
5.4
)), we can find a new diffeomorphism
\varphi_{A}
such that for
h=\eta\left(\varphi_{A}^{*}g_{1}-\bar{g}\right),\chi=\eta\left(\varphi_{A}^{*}f_{1}-\bar{f}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\bar{b}\leq L-6}\rVert\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\rVert_{C^{2,\frac{1}{2}}}+\left|\mathcal{B}(h,\chi)\right|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{(L-6)^{2}}{8}-\frac{L^{2}}{34}}.

Set
g_{2}=\bar{g}+h,f_{2}=\bar{f}+\chi
and write
h=ug_{S^{m}}+\zeta,\chi=mu/2+q
, where
ug_{S^{m}}
is the projection of
h
to
\mathcal{K}_{0}g_{S^{m}}
. Note that
h
and
\chi
are both supported on
\{\bar{b}<L\}
, then by Theorem
4.17
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{W}(g_{2},f_{2})-\Theta_{m}\right|\leq C(n)\left(\alpha^{3}+\mathfrak{X}^{2}+\left|\mathcal{V}(g_{2},f_{2})-1\right|\right),

(5.20)

where
\alpha=\rVert u\rVert_{L^{2}}
and
\mathfrak{X}=\|\mathbf{\Phi}(g_{2},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathrm{div}_{\bar{f}}h\|_{W^{1,2}}+|\mathcal{B}(h,\chi)|
. By Theorem
2.13
(i) again, we know that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{V}(g_{2},f_{2})-1\right|\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{-\frac{(1-\epsilon)L^{2}}{4}}.

(5.21)

As in the proof of Proposition
5.2
(see (
5.7
) and (
5.8
)), we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}+\alpha^{2}\leq C(n)e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{8}}.

(5.22)

Plugging (
5.21
) and (
5.22
) into (
5.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{W}(g_{2},f_{2})-\Theta_{m}\right|\leq C(n)(e^{-\frac{3L^{2}}{16}}+e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{4}})+C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{-\frac{(1-\epsilon)L^{2}}{4}}\leq C(n,Y)e^{-\frac{3L^{2}}{16}},

(5.23)

provided that
\epsilon
is chosen to be small.

By constructions of
\varphi_{A}
and
\varphi_{B}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{W}(g_{2},f_{2})-\mathcal{W}(g_{1},f_{1})\right|\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{-\frac{(1-\epsilon)L^{2}}{4}}.

(5.24)

Combining (
5.19
), (
5.23
) and (
5.24
), we finally conclude by choosing a small
\epsilon>0
that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{W}(g_{0},f_{0})-\Theta_{m}\right|\leq C(n,Y)e^{-\frac{3L^{2}}{16}}.

Since
L=\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}
and
\mathcal{W}(g_{0},f_{0})=\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1)
, this completes the proof.
∎

6
Lojasiewicz inequalities for cylindrical geometries

In this section, we consider a closed Ricci flow
\mathcal{X}=\{M^{n},(g(t))_{t\in I}\}
with entropy bounded below by
-Y
such that
[-10,0]\subset I
. Throughout, we fix a spacetime point
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},0)
, define
\tau=-t
, and set

\displaystyle\begin{cases}&\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\mathrm{d}\nu_{x^{*}_{0};t}=(4\pi\tau)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-f}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)},\\
&\Phi=\frac{g}{2}-\tau\left(\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}f\right),\\
&F=\tau f.\end{cases}

Moreover, we define
b=2\sqrt{\left|f(-1)\right|}
.

As before, the model space we consider is the weighted cylinder:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{n-m}_{-1}=(\bar{M},\bar{g}(-1),\bar{f}(-1))=\left(\mathbb{R}^{n-m}\times S^{m},g_{E}\times g_{S^{m}},\frac{|\vec{x}|^{2}}{4}+\frac{m}{2}+\Theta_{m}\right),

where
m\in\{2,\ldots,n-1\}
. We set
(\bar{M},\bar{g}(t),\bar{f}(t))
to be the induced Ricci flow such that
t=0
is the singular time coupled with
\bar{f}(t):=|\vec{x}|^{2}/4\tau+m/2+\Theta_{m}
. We set
\bar{F}=|\vec{x}|^{2}/4
,
\bar{b}=2\sqrt{\bar{f}(-1)}
, and fix
\bar{p}
to be a minimum point of
\bar{b}
.

Definition 6.1

(
\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}
-radius)
.

For the weighted Riemannian manifold
\left(M,g(-1),f(-1)\right)
,

\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}
is defined as the largest
L
such that there exists a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{C}
from
\{\bar{b}\leq L\}
of
\bar{M}
onto a subset of
M
such that
f\left(\varphi_{C}(\bar{p}),-1\right)\leq n
and

\displaystyle\left[\bar{g}-\varphi_{C}^{*}g(-1)\right]_{2}\leq\delta.

Proposition 6.2
.

For any small
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}(n,\epsilon)>0
such that if
\delta\leq\bar{\delta}
and
\mathbf{r}_{C,\delta}\geq\bar{\delta}^{-2}
, then

\displaystyle\left[\varphi_{C}^{*}g(t)-\bar{g}(t)\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}\leq\epsilon

on
\left\{\bar{b}\leq\mathbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\right\}\times[-9,-\epsilon]
, where the norm
[\cdot]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}
is taken with respect to
\bar{g}(t)
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we set
L=\mathbf{r}_{C,\delta}
and
g_{0}(t)=\varphi_{C}^{*}g(t)
, which is regarded as a Ricci flow on
\{\bar{b}\leq L\}\times[-10,0]
.

It follows from the two-sided pseudolocality theorem (see Theorem
2.14
) and Shi’s local estimates (see
[
Shi89
]
) that for a fixed
\epsilon>0
, there exist constants
\theta=\theta(n,\epsilon)\in(0,\epsilon)
such that if

\displaystyle\left[g_{0}(t)-\bar{g}(t)\right]_{2}\leq\theta

on
\{\bar{b}\leq r\}
, for some
r\in(1,L)
and some
t\in[-9,-\epsilon]
, then

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{k}\mathrm{Rm}(g_{0})\right|\leq C(n,k,\epsilon)

(6.1)

holds on the region
\left\{\bar{b}\leq r-1\right\}\times[t-10\theta,t+2\theta]
.

We first claim that

\displaystyle\left[g_{0}(t)-\bar{g}(t)\right]_{[\theta^{-1}]}\leq\theta

on
\left\{\bar{b}\leq L-\bar{\delta}^{-1}/2\right\}\times[-1-9\theta,-1+\theta]
, provided that
\bar{\delta}
is sufficiently small. Suppose, for contradiction, that the conclusion fails. Then, there exist sequences
L_{i}\to+\infty
and
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
with
L_{i}\geq\epsilon_{i}^{-2}
, and a sequence of Ricci flows
g_{i}(t)
such that

\displaystyle\left[g_{i}(-1)-\bar{g}(-1)\right]_{2}\leq\epsilon_{i}

(6.2)

on
\{\bar{b}\leq L_{i}\}
. By (
6.1
), we obtain for large
i

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{k}\mathrm{Rm}(g_{i}(t))\right|\leq C(n,k,\epsilon)

(6.3)

on the region
\left\{\bar{b}\leq L_{i}-1\right\}\times[t-10\theta,t+2\theta]
. By our assumption, there exist
q_{i}
with
\bar{b}(q_{i})\leq L_{i}-\epsilon_{i}^{-1}/2
and
t_{i}\in[-1-9\theta,-1+\theta]
such that

\displaystyle\left[g_{i}(t_{i})-\bar{g}(t_{i})\right]_{[\theta^{-1}]}(q_{i})>\theta.

(6.4)

It follows from (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) that, after taking a subsequence, the following convergence holds for
t\in(t-10\theta,t+2\theta)
:

\displaystyle\left(\bar{M},g_{i}(t),q_{i}\right)\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{\hskip 8.19447ptC_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}\hskip 8.19447pt}\left(\bar{M},g_{\infty}(t),q_{\infty}\right),

where the smooth convergence is in the fixed coordinate system given by
\bar{M}
. Moreover,
g_{\infty}(-1)=\bar{g}(-1)
. By the two-sided uniqueness of the Ricci flow (see
[
CZ06
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
Kot10
, Theorem 1]
), this implies that
g_{\infty}(t)=\bar{g}(t)
for any
t\in[-1-9\theta,-1+\theta]
. However, this contradicts (
6.4
) if
i
is sufficiently large.

By the same argument with adjusting constants and induction, we can prove that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
with
j\leq(1-\epsilon)/\theta
, we have

\displaystyle\left[g_{0}(t)-\bar{g}(t)\right]_{[\theta^{-1}]}\leq\theta

on
\left\{\bar{b}\leq L-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\left(2^{-1}+\cdots+2^{-j}\right)\right\}\times[-1-9j\theta,-1+j\theta]
, provided that
\bar{\delta}
is sufficiently small.

Since
\theta<\epsilon
, this completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 6.3
.

Under the same assumptions of Proposition
6.2
, we have

(1-100\epsilon)\bar{F}(x)-C(n,Y,\epsilon)\leq F(\varphi_{C}(x),t)\leq(1+100\epsilon)\bar{F}(x)+C(n,Y,\epsilon)

(6.5)

for
(x,t)\in\left\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\right\}\times[-8,-1/8]
.

Proof.

We set
p=\varphi_{C}(\bar{p})
. By Definition
6.1
, since
f\left(p,-1\right)\leq n
, it follows from Theorem
2.13
(ii) that
(p,-1)
is an
H(n,Y)
-center of
x_{0}^{*}
.

Claim 6.4
.

For any
t\in[-9,-\epsilon]
,
(p,t)
is an
H
-center of
x_{0}^{*}
, where
H=H(n,Y,|t|)>0
is a constant.

For
t\in[-9,-1)
, it follows from Proposition
6.2
that
|\mathrm{Rm}|\leq C(n)
along
{p}\times[t,-1]
. Hence, by
[
FL25a
, Proposition 2.21(i)]
, we conclude that
(p,t)
is an
H(n,Y)
-center of
(p,-1)
.
By the monotonicity property (see
[
FL25a
, Proposition 2.12]
), we have

\displaystyle C(n,Y)\geq d_{W_{1}}^{-1}(\nu_{-1},\delta_{p})\geq d_{W_{1}}^{t}(\nu_{t},\nu_{p,-1;t})\geq d_{W_{1}}^{t}(\nu_{t},\delta_{p})-d_{W_{1}}^{t}(\delta_{p},\nu_{p,-1;t})\geq d_{W_{1}}^{t}(\nu_{t},\delta_{p})-C(n,Y),

from which it follows that
(p,t)
is an
H(n,Y)
-center of
x_{0}^{*}
.

For
t\in(-1,-\epsilon]
, by the reproduction formula we have

\displaystyle K(x_{0},0;p,-1)=\int_{M}K(w,t;p,-1)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}(w).

Since
K(x_{0},0;p,-1)\geq c(n)>0
by assumption, it follows from Theorem
2.13
, Proposition
6.2
, and
[
Bam23
, Proposition 9.16(b)]
that any
H_{n}
-center
(z,t)
of
x_{0}^{*}
must satisfy

\displaystyle d_{t}(p,z)\leq C(n,Y,|t|),

which completes the proof of Claim
6.4
.

In particular,
(p,t)
is an
H(n,Y)
-center of
x_{0}^{*}
for any
t\in[-8,-1/8]
. By Theorem
2.13
(ii), we conclude that

\displaystyle K(x_{0},0;x,t)\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)\tau^{-\frac{n}{2}}\exp\left(-\frac{d_{t}^{2}(p,x)}{4(1+\epsilon)\tau}\right)

(6.6)

for any
(x,t)\in M\times[-8,-1/8]
. Since
K(x_{0},0;\cdot,t)=(4\pi\tau)^{-n/2}e^{-f}
, it follows from (
6.6
) that for any
(x,t)\in M\times[-8,-1/8]
,

\displaystyle f(x,t)\geq\frac{d_{t}^{2}(p,x)}{4(1+\epsilon)\tau}-C(n,Y,\epsilon),

which implies on
M\times[-8,-1/8]
,

\displaystyle F(x,t)\geq\frac{d_{t}^{2}(p,x)}{4(1+\epsilon)}-C(n,Y,\epsilon).

(6.7)

Claim 6.5
.

For any
(x,t)\in\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\times[-9,-\epsilon]
,

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon)\bar{d}_{t}(\bar{p},x)\leq d_{t}(p,\varphi_{C}(x))\leq(1+\epsilon)\bar{d}_{t}(\bar{p},x).

(6.8)

The upper bound in (
6.8
) is straightforward. Indeed, fix
t\in[-9,-\epsilon]
and let
\tilde{\gamma}
be a minimizing geodesic from
\bar{p}
to
x
with respect to
\bar{g}(t)
. Since
x\in\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}
, the entire curve
\tilde{\gamma}
lies in
\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}\}
. By Proposition
6.2
, the length of
\varphi_{C}(\tilde{\gamma})
with respect to
g(t)
is at most
(1+\epsilon)\bar{d}_{t}(\bar{p},x)
, yielding the upper bound.

For the lower bound in (
6.8
), fix
t\in[-9,-\epsilon]
and let
\gamma(s)
for
s\in[0,L]
be a minimizing geodesic from
p
to
\varphi_{C}(x)
with respect to
g(t)
, where
L=d_{t}(p,\varphi_{C}(x))
. If
\gamma
lies entirely in
\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}\}\right)
, then applying
\varphi_{C}^{-1}
and using the previous argument gives the lower bound. Otherwise, let
s_{0}
be the smallest
s\in[0,L]
for which
\gamma(s_{0})
lies on the boundary of
\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}\}\right)
. Define
\gamma^{\prime}=\varphi_{C}^{-1}(\gamma|_{[0,s_{0}]})
. By the definition of
s_{0}
, its length with respect to
\bar{g}(t)
is at least
\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}
. Thus, by Proposition
6.2
,

\displaystyle d_{t}(p,\varphi_{C}(x))\geq(1-\epsilon)\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}\geq(1-\epsilon)\bar{d}_{t}(\bar{p},x),

which establishes the lower bound in (
6.8
) and hence finishes the proof of Claim
6.5
.

By the definition of
\bar{F}
, it is easy to show that for any
(x,t)\in\bar{M}\times[-9,-\epsilon]
,

\displaystyle\bar{F}(x)\leq\frac{\bar{d}_{t}^{2}(\bar{p},x)}{4}\leq\bar{F}(x)+C(n).

(6.9)

Combining (
6.7
), (
6.8
) and (
6.9
), we conclude that

\displaystyle F(\varphi_{C}(x),t)\geq

\displaystyle\frac{d_{t}^{2}(p,\varphi_{C}(x))}{4(1+\epsilon)}-C(n,Y,\epsilon)\geq\frac{(1-\epsilon)^{2}}{4(1+\epsilon)}\bar{d}_{t}^{2}(\bar{p},x)-C(n,Y,\epsilon)\geq\frac{(1-\epsilon)^{2}}{1+\epsilon}\bar{F}(x)-C(n,Y,\epsilon),

which implies the lower bound in (
6.5
).

For the upper bound in (
6.5
), we first choose an
H_{n}
-center
(z,-\epsilon)
of
x_{0}^{*}
. From the reproduction formula, we have for
(x,t)\in M\times[-8,-1/8]
,

\displaystyle K(x_{0},0;x,t)\geq\int_{B_{-\epsilon}(z,\sqrt{2H_{n}\epsilon})}K(w,-\epsilon;x,t)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-\epsilon}(w).

(6.10)

Since
(p,-\epsilon)
is an
H(n,Y,\epsilon)
-center of
x_{0}^{*}
by Claim
6.4
, it follows that

\displaystyle d_{-\epsilon}(p,z)\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon).

(6.11)

For
w\in B_{-\epsilon}\big(z,\sqrt{2H_{n}\epsilon}\big)
, we claim that for any
(x,t)\in\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\right)\times[-8,-1/8]
, it holds that

\displaystyle K(w,-\epsilon;x,t)\geq c(n,Y,\epsilon)\exp\left(-\frac{d^{2}_{-\epsilon}(w,x)}{4(1-\epsilon)(-\epsilon-t)}\right)>0.

(6.12)

In fact, by
[
Per02
, Corollary 9.5]
, we have

\displaystyle K(x,-\epsilon;x,t)\geq\left(4\pi(-\epsilon-t)\right)^{-n/2}e^{-l_{x,-\epsilon}(x,t)}\geq c(n)e^{-l_{x,-\epsilon}(x,t)}>0,

where
l_{x,-\epsilon}(x,t)
is the reduced distance. By Proposition
6.2
,
(1-t)|\mathrm{Rm}|\leq C(n)
along
\{x\}\times[-9,-\epsilon]
, thus,

\displaystyle l_{x,-\epsilon}(x,t)\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{-\epsilon-t}}\int_{t}^{-\epsilon}\sqrt{-\epsilon-s}\mathrm{R}(x,s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(n),

which implies

\displaystyle K(x,-\epsilon;x,t)\geq c(n)>0.

(6.13)

By the same argument as in
[
LW20
, Corollary 6]
and Theorem
2.13
(ii), we see that for any
A>1
,

\displaystyle K(x,-\epsilon;x,t)\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon,A)K(w,-\epsilon;x,t)^{\frac{1}{1+A}}\exp\left(\frac{d^{2}_{-\epsilon}(w,x)}{4A(-\epsilon-t)}\right).

Combined with (
6.13
), we obtain

\displaystyle K(w,-\epsilon;x,t)\geq c(n,Y,A,\epsilon)\exp\left(-\frac{(1+A)d^{2}_{-\epsilon}(w,x)}{4A(-\epsilon-t)}\right)>0.

By choosing
A:=\epsilon^{-1}
, we obtain (
6.12
).

Combining (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), it follows that for
(x,t)\in\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\right)\times[-8,-1/8]
,

\displaystyle K(w,-\epsilon;x,t)\geq c(n,Y,\epsilon)\exp\left(-\frac{d^{2}_{-\epsilon}(p,x)}{4(1-\epsilon)(-\epsilon-t)}\right)>0,

(6.14)

by slightly adjusting
\epsilon
. From (
6.10
), (
6.14
) and the fact that
\nu_{-\epsilon}\left(B_{-\epsilon}(z,\sqrt{2H_{n}\epsilon})\right)\geq 1/2
(cf. Proposition
2.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle K(x_{0},0;x,t)\geq c(n,Y,\epsilon)\exp\left(-\frac{d^{2}_{-\epsilon}(p,x)}{4(1-\epsilon)(-\epsilon-t)}\right)>0

for
(x,t)\in\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\right)\times[-8,-1/8]
. Combining this with (
6.8
) and (
6.9
), we obtain for
(x,t)\in\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\times[-8,-1/8]

\displaystyle F(\varphi_{C}(x),t)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(-t)}{(1-\epsilon)(-\epsilon-t)}\frac{d^{2}_{-\epsilon}(p,\varphi_{C}(x))}{4}+C(n,Y,\epsilon)\leq\frac{(1+\epsilon)^{2}(-t)}{(1-\epsilon)(-\epsilon-t)}\frac{\bar{d}^{2}_{-\epsilon}(\bar{p},x)}{4}+C(n,Y,\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(1+\epsilon)^{2}(-t)}{(1-\epsilon)(-\epsilon-t)}\bar{F}(x)+C(n,Y,\epsilon)\leq(1+100\epsilon)\bar{F}(x)+C(n,Y,\epsilon),

which yields the upper bound in (
6.5
).

In sum, the proof is complete.
∎

Corollary 6.6
.

Under the same assumptions of Proposition
6.2
, for any
1\leq l\leq\epsilon^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle[\varphi_{C}^{*}F]_{l}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)\exp\left(10^{5}\epsilon l\bar{F}\right)

on
\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-2\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\times[-4,-1/4]
.

Proof.

We set
K=K(x_{0},0;\cdot,\cdot)=(4\pi\tau)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-f}
. Then it is clear from
-\partial_{t}K=\Delta K-\mathrm{R}K
and the standard interior estimates that if
\delta\leq\bar{\delta}
, then for any
(x,t)\in\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-2\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\right)\times[-4,-1/4]
,

\displaystyle\rVert K\rVert_{C^{l}\left(B_{t}(x,1)\times(t-\epsilon,t+\epsilon)\right)}\leq C(n,\epsilon,l)\rVert K\rVert_{C^{0}\left(B_{t}(x,2)\times(t-2\epsilon,t+2\epsilon)\right)}.

Thus, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\sum_{i_{1}+\cdots+i_{k}=l}\nabla^{i_{1}}f*\cdots*\nabla^{i_{k}}f(x,t)\right\rvert\leq C(n,\epsilon,l)\exp\left(f(x,t)-\inf_{B_{t}(x,2)\times(t-2\epsilon,t+2\epsilon)}f\right).

(6.15)

From Proposition
6.3
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|f(x,t)-\inf_{B_{t}(x,2)\times(t-2\epsilon,t+2\epsilon)}f\right|\leq 10^{5}\epsilon\bar{F}(\varphi_{C}^{-1}(x))+C(n,Y,\epsilon).

(6.16)

Combining (
6.15
) with (
6.16
), it follows from induction that on
\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-2\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\right)\times[-4,-1/4]
,

\displaystyle[F]_{l}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon,l)\exp\left(10^{5}\epsilon l\bar{F}\circ\varphi_{C}^{-1}\right),

for any
l\geq 1
. Thus, the conclusion holds from Proposition
6.2
.
∎

Next, we define a function
\tilde{F}
on
M
such that

\displaystyle\square\tilde{F}=-\frac{n}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{F}=F\quad\text{on}\quad t=-2.

(6.17)

Proposition 6.7
.

We have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[-2,-1/2]}\int_{M}\left|\nabla(\tilde{F}-F)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}+\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}(\tilde{F}-F)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 6\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2)\right).

In particular,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left|\nabla(\tilde{F}-F)\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{-1}+\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\left|\frac{g}{2}-\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}-\tau\mathrm{Ric}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 7\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2)\right).

Proof.

We set
w=\tau(2\Delta f-|\nabla f|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f-n
and
u=\tilde{F}-F
. Then the following evolution equation holds from (
2.4
):

\displaystyle\square u=w\quad\text{and}\quad u=0\quad\mathrm{at}\quad t=-2.

By the weighted Bianchi identity (see Lemma
2.5
), we calculate

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{M}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\square\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=2\int_{M}\langle\nabla\square u,\nabla u\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=2\int_{M}\langle\nabla w,\nabla u\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=4\int_{M}\langle\mathrm{div}_{f}\mathcal{T},\nabla u\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}-2\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t},

where
\mathcal{T}=\tau\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}(\tau f)-\frac{g}{2}
. Using integration by parts and integrating in time, we obtain that for any
t_{1}\in[-2,0]
,

\displaystyle 2\int_{-2}^{t_{1}}\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=-4\int_{-2}^{t_{1}}\int_{M}\langle\mathcal{T},\nabla^{2}u\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{M}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}\leq\int_{-2}^{t_{1}}\int_{M}4\left|\mathcal{T}\right|^{2}+\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t

(6.18)

and thus we get

\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 4\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\left|\mathcal{T}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

(6.19)

Note that by Proposition
2.8
(iii),

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2)=2\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\tau^{-1}\left|\mathcal{T}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\geq\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\left|\mathcal{T}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

Combining this with (
6.19
) and using the definition of
u
and
\mathcal{T}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\left|\tau\mathrm{Ric}+\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}-\frac{g}{2}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 5\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}\left|\mathcal{T}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 5\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2)\right).

Moreover, by (
6.18
), for any
t_{1}\in[-2,-1/2]
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq-4\int_{-2}^{t_{1}}\int_{M}\langle\mathcal{T},\nabla^{2}u\rangle\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t-2\int_{-2}^{t_{1}}\int_{M}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{-2}^{-1/2}\int_{M}|\mathcal{T}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq 2\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2)\right).

Thus, it is clear that the estimates hold.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

In the same setting as Proposition
6.2
, we have

(1-300\epsilon)\bar{F}(x)-C(n,Y,\epsilon)\leq\tilde{F}(\varphi_{C}(x),t)\leq(1+300\epsilon)\bar{F}(x)+C(n,Y,\epsilon).

(6.20)

for any
(x,t)\in\left\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\right\}\times[-2,-1/2]
. Moreover, for any
1\leq l\leq\epsilon^{-1}
, we have on
\{\bar{b}\leq\mathbf{r}_{C,\delta}-2\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\times[-2,-1/2]
,

\displaystyle[\varphi_{C}^{*}\tilde{F}]_{l}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)\exp\left(10^{5}\epsilon l\bar{F}\right).

Proof.

Since
w=\tau(2\Delta f-|\nabla f|^{2}+\mathrm{R})+f-n\leq 0
, it follows from

\displaystyle\square F=-\frac{n}{2}-w\geq\square\tilde{F},

and the maximum principle that

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(x,t)\leq F(x,t)

for any
(x,t)\in M\times[-2,0]
. By Proposition
6.3
, this yields the upper bound of (
6.20
).

On the other hand, for any
(x,t)\in\varphi_{C}\left(\{\bar{b}\leq\mathbf{r}_{C,\delta}-\bar{\delta}^{-1}\}\right)\times[-2,-1/2]
and any its
H_{n}
-center
(z,-2)
, it follows from
\square\left(\tilde{F}+nt/2\right)=0
that

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(x,t)+\frac{n}{2}t=\int_{M}F(\cdot,-2)-n\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x,t;-2}\geq\int_{B_{-2}\left(z,\sqrt{\epsilon^{-1}H_{n}(t+2)}\right)}F(\cdot,-2)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{x,t;-2}-C(n,Y),

(6.21)

where we used the fact
F(\cdot,-2)\geq-C(n,Y)
, which follows from Theorem
2.13
(ii).

By Proposition
2.12
, we have

\displaystyle\nu_{x,t;-2}\left(B_{-2}\big(z,\sqrt{\epsilon^{-1}H_{n}(t+2)}\big)\right)\geq 1-\epsilon.

(6.22)

From Proposition
6.2
and
[
FL25a
, Proposition 2.21(i)]
, we have
d_{-2}(x,z)\leq C(n,Y)
. Thus, for
y\in B_{-2}\left(z,\sqrt{\epsilon^{-1}H_{n}(t+2)}\right)
, it follows from Proposition
6.3
that

\displaystyle F(y,-2)\geq(1-100\epsilon)\bar{F}(\varphi_{C}^{-1}(y))-C(n,Y,\epsilon)\geq(1-200\epsilon)\bar{F}(\varphi_{C}^{-1}(x))-C(n,Y,\epsilon).

(6.23)

Plugging (
6.22
) and (
6.23
) into (
6.21
), it follows that

\displaystyle\tilde{F}(x,t)\geq(1-300\epsilon)\bar{F}(\varphi_{C}^{-1}(x))-C(n,Y,\epsilon).

Therefore, we obtain the lower bound for
\tilde{F}
in (
6.20
). Since
\square\left(\tilde{F}+nt/2\right)=0
, the higher order estimates follow from standard parabolic regularity and a similar argument as in Corollary
6.6
.
∎

Definition 6.9

(Entropy radius)
.

The
entropy radius

\mathbf{r}_{E}
is defined as

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\mathbf{r}^{2}_{E}}{4}\right):=\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2).

Here, we implicitly assume that
\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(1/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2)<1
so that
\mathbf{r}_{E}
is well-defined.

We now fix a small constant
\sigma\in(0,1/10)
, an integer
l\in\mathbb{N}
and define:

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}_{l}:=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\delta}\left(n,10^{-100}n^{-1}l^{-1}\sigma\right),

for
l\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\bar{\delta}
is the function defined in Proposition
6.2
.

In the following, we always assume
\textbf{r}_{C,\bar{\delta}_{l}}\geq\bar{\delta}_{l}^{-2}
, and we identify
g
,
f
,
F
, and
\tilde{F}
with their pullbacks
\varphi_{C}^{*}g
,
\varphi_{C}^{*}f
,
\varphi_{C}^{*}F
, and
\varphi_{C}^{*}\tilde{F}
, respectively, where
\varphi_{C}
is the diffeomorphism corresponding to
\textbf{r}_{C,\bar{\delta}_{l}}
in Definition
6.1
. Furthermore, we set

\displaystyle\bar{L}:=\textbf{r}_{C,\bar{\delta}_{l}}-\bar{\delta}_{l}^{-1}

so that the preceding estimates hold on the set
\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}\}\times[-2,-1/2]
with constant
\epsilon
replaced by
10^{-100}n^{-1}l^{-1}\sigma
.

Lemma 6.10
.

If
\bar{L}\leq(1-\sigma)\mathbf{r}_{E}
, then on
\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-1\}\times[-2,-1/2]
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\nabla(\tilde{F}-F)\right]_{l}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{2}-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{8(1-\sigma)}}.

(6.24)

Proof.

By the definition of
\bar{L}
and Lemma
6.7
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[-2,-1/2]}\int_{M}\left|\nabla(F-\tilde{F})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq Ce^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}}.

(6.25)

By Corollary
6.6
and Lemma
6.8
, we have

\displaystyle\left[F-\tilde{F}\right]_{i}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\exp\left(10^{-90}in^{-1}l^{-1}\sigma\bar{L}^{2}\right)

(6.26)

on
\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-1\}\times[-2,-1/2]
, for any
0\leq i\leq 10^{20}n\sigma^{-1}l
.

For any
(x,t)\in\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}-1\}\times[-2,-1/2]
, we set
B=B_{t}(x,1/2)
and
2B=B_{t}(x,1)
. Applying the interpolation inequality
[
CMI15
, Lemma B.1]
to
U=|\nabla(F-\tilde{F})|^{2}e^{-f}
, we get

\displaystyle\rVert U\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B)}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,k)\left(\rVert U\rVert_{L^{1}(2B)}+\rVert U\rVert_{L^{1}(2B)}^{a_{k,n}}\rVert\nabla^{k}U\rVert_{L^{\infty}(2B)}^{1-a_{k,n}}\right),

(6.27)

where the norms are unweighted. By (
6.25
),
\rVert U\rVert_{L^{1}(B)}\leq Ce^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}}
. Moreover, it follows from (
6.26
) and Corollary
6.6
that for
k=10^{10}n\sigma^{-1}l
,

\displaystyle\rVert\nabla^{k}U\rVert_{L^{\infty}(2B)}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\exp\left(\bar{L}^{2}-\inf_{2B}f\right).

Plugging these estimates into (
6.27
), it follows that

\displaystyle\rVert U\rVert_{L^{\infty}(B)}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\left(\exp\left(-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}\right)+\exp\left(-\frac{a_{k,n}\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}+(1-a_{k,n})(\bar{L}^{2}-\inf_{2B}f)\right)\right).

(6.28)

Since

\displaystyle 1-a_{k,n}=\frac{n}{k+n}=\frac{1}{10^{10}\sigma^{-1}l+1},

it is clear by a direct calculation that

\displaystyle-\frac{a_{k,n}\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}+(1-a_{k,n})\bar{L}^{2}\leq-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-3\sigma/2)}.

Note that by Proposition
6.3
,

\displaystyle\left|f(x,t)-\inf_{2B}f\right|+\left|f(x,t)-\bar{f}(x,t)\right|\leq 10^{-90}n^{-1}l^{-1}\sigma\bar{L}^{2}.

Thus, (
6.28
) yields:

\displaystyle\left|\nabla(F-\tilde{F})\right|\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\exp\left(\frac{f}{2}-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{8(1-3\sigma/2)}\right)\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\exp\left(\frac{\bar{f}}{2}-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{8(1-1.1\sigma)}\right).

(6.29)

Consequently, (
6.24
) follows from the interpolation based on (
6.26
) and (
6.29
).

∎

Lemma 6.11
.

If
\bar{L}\leq(1-\sigma)\mathbf{r}_{E}
, then for any
t\in[-3/2,-1/2]
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-1\right\}}\left|\frac{g}{2}-\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}-\tau\mathrm{Ric}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n)e^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}}.

Proof.

We set
\tilde{\Phi}:=g/2-\tau\mathrm{Ric}-\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}
. It follows from a direct calculation that
\left(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{L}\right)\tilde{\Phi}=0
, where

\displaystyle\left(\Delta_{L}h\right)_{ij}:=\Delta h_{ij}+2\mathrm{Rm}_{ikjl}h_{kl}-\mathrm{Ric}_{ik}h_{jk}-\mathrm{Ric}_{jk}h_{ik}

denotes the Lichnerowicz Laplacian with respect to
g
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}-\Delta)|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\leq-2|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+C(n)|\mathrm{Rm}||\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}.

(6.30)

Choose a cut-off function
\eta
such that
\eta=1
on
\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}-1\}
and
\eta=0
outside
\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}\}
. By Lemma
6.2
, we may assume that
|\nabla\eta|+|\nabla^{2}\eta|\leq C(n)
. Moreover,
|\mathrm{Rm}|
is bounded by
C(n)
on the support of
\eta
.

According to (
6.30
), we obtain

\displaystyle\square\left(\eta^{2}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-2\eta^{2}|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+C(n)\eta^{2}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}-\left(\Delta\eta^{2}\right)|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}-2\langle\nabla\eta^{2},\nabla|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-2\eta^{2}|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+C(n)\chi_{\mathrm{supp}(\eta)}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+2\eta^{2}|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+8|\nabla\eta|^{2}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\leq C(n)\chi_{\mathrm{supp}(\eta)}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2},

where
\chi_{\mathrm{supp}(\eta)}
denotes the characteristic function of
\mathrm{supp}(\eta)
, and in the second inequality, we have used

\displaystyle\left|2\langle\nabla\eta^{2},\nabla|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\rangle\right|\leq 8|\eta||\nabla\eta||\tilde{\Phi}||\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|\leq 2\eta^{2}|\nabla\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}+8|\nabla\eta|^{2}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{M}\eta^{2}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}=\int_{M}\square(\eta^{2}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2})\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n)\int_{\mathrm{supp}(\eta)}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}.

For any
t\in[-3/2,-1/2]
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}-1\right\}}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq

\displaystyle\min_{t\in[-2,-\frac{3}{2}]}\int_{\left\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}\right\}}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}+C(n)\int_{-2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\left\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}\right\}}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(n)\int_{-2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\left\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}\right\}}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t.

(6.31)

By Proposition
6.7
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{-2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\left\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}\right\}}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\mathrm{d}t\leq Ce^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}},

which, by (
6
), implies that for any
t\in[-3/2,-1/2]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\bar{b}<\bar{L}-1\right\}}|\tilde{\Phi}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n)e^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Combining Lemma
6.10
and Lemma
6.11
, we obtain the following time slice estimate:

Corollary 6.12
.

If
\bar{L}\leq(1-\sigma)\mathbf{r}_{E}
, then for any
t\in[-3/2,-1/2]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-1\right\}}\left\lvert\frac{g}{2}-\mathrm{Ric}-\nabla^{2}F\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)e^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.3
and Lemma
6.10
, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}(\tilde{F}-F)|^{2}e^{-f}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\exp\left(10^{-90}n^{-1}l^{-1}\sigma\bar{L}^{2}-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)}\right)\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\exp\left(-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-0.5\sigma)}\right),

which implies for any
t\in[-3/2,-1/2]
,

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-1\right\}}|\nabla^{2}(\tilde{F}-F)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)\bar{L}^{n}\exp\left(-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-0.5\sigma)}\right)\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)e^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4}}.

(6.32)

Consequently, the conclusion follows from Lemma
6.11
and (
6.32
).
∎

Proposition 6.13
.

If
\bar{L}\leq(1-\sigma)\mathbf{r}_{E}
, then on
\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-2\}\times[-3/2,-1/2]
,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{g}{2}-\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}-\tau\mathrm{Ric}\right]_{l}+\left[\frac{g}{2}-\nabla^{2}F-\tau\mathrm{Ric}\right]_{l}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{2}-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{8(1-\sigma)}}.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the conclusion for
[g/2-\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}-\tau\mathrm{Ric}]_{l}
since the estimate for
[g/2-\nabla^{2}F-\tau\mathrm{Ric}]_{l}
follows from Lemma
6.10
. By Lemma
6.11
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\left\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-1\right\}}\left|\frac{g}{2}-\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}-\tau\mathrm{Ric}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{t}\leq C(n)e^{-\frac{\bar{L}^{2}}{4(1-\sigma)^{2}}}.

(6.33)

On the other hand, it follows from Proposition
6.2
and Lemma
6.8
that on
\{\bar{b}\leq\bar{L}-2\}\times[-3/2,-1/2]
,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{g}{2}-\nabla^{2}\tilde{F}-\tau\mathrm{Ric}\right]_{10^{10}n\sigma^{-1}l-2}\leq C(n,Y,\sigma,l)e^{\bar{L}^{2}}.

(6.34)

Combining (
6.33
) and (
6.34
), we can follow the interpolation argument as in the proof of Lemma
6.10
to conclude the proof.
∎

Comparison of radius functions II

Next, we compare the radius functions
\textbf{r}_{A}
and
\mathbf{r}_{C,\bar{\delta}_{l}}
and their corresponding diffeomorphisms. Here,
\textbf{r}_{A},\textbf{r}_{C,\delta}
denote the radius functions for
(M,g(-1),f(-1))
.

Theorem 6.14
.

For any
\sigma\in(0,1/10)
,
D>1
and
l\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a large constant
L^{\prime}=L^{\prime}(n,Y,\sigma,l,D)>1
satisfying the following property.

Let
\varphi_{A}
be a diffeomorphism corresponding to
\textbf{r}_{A}
in Definition
5.1
. If
\mathbf{r}_{A}\in[L^{\prime},(1-\sigma)\mathbf{r}_{E}]
, then there exists another diffeomorphism
\varphi
from
\{\bar{b}\leq\mathbf{r}_{A}+D\}
onto a subset of
M
such that
\varphi=\varphi_{A}
on
\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{A}-2\bar{\delta}_{l}^{-1}\}
and

\displaystyle\left[\varphi^{*}g(-1)-\bar{g}\right]_{2}\leq\bar{\delta}_{l}

on
\{\bar{b}\leq\mathbf{r}_{A}+D\}
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{C,\bar{\delta}_{l}}\geq\mathbf{r}_{A}+D.

Proof.

By our definition of
\bar{\delta}_{l}
, the conclusions of Lemma
6.10
, Lemma
6.11
, and Proposition
6.13
hold.

By the definitions of
\textbf{r}_{A}
and
\textbf{r}_{C,\bar{\delta}_{l}}
, we conclude that
\textbf{r}_{C,\bar{\delta}_{l}}\geq\mathbf{r}_{A}-1
, provided that
L^{\prime}
is sufficiently large.

Suppose the conclusion fails. Then there exists a sequence of Ricci flows
\mathcal{X}^{i}=\{M^{n}_{i},(g_{i}(t))_{t\in[-10,0]}\}
with entropy bounded below by
-Y
. Moreover, there exist base points
x_{0,i}^{*}=(x_{0,i},0)\in\mathcal{X}^{i}
such that
\textbf{r}_{A}^{i}\to+\infty
and
\textbf{r}_{A}^{i}\leq(1-\sigma)\mathbf{r}^{i}_{E}
. For each
i
, there exists a diffeomorphism
\varphi_{A,i}
from
\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{A}^{i}\}
onto a subset of
M
corresponding to
\textbf{r}_{A}^{i}
in Definition
5.1
. However, it is not possible to find a diffeomorphism from
\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{A}^{i}+D_{0}\}\to M_{i}
for some constant
D_{0}>0
that satisfies the required properties. Here and throughout, we use subscript or superscript
i
to indicate the corresponding quantities associated with
\mathcal{X}^{i}
.

We define
D_{1}:=D_{0}+2\bar{\delta}_{l}^{-1}
and
L_{i}:=\textbf{r}_{A}^{i}-2\bar{\delta}_{l}^{-1}
. Then, we define the hypersurface
\Sigma_{i}:=\varphi_{A,i}\left(\{\bar{b}=L_{i}\}\right)
. Moreover, we choose a base point
q_{i}^{*}=(q_{i},-1/2)\in\mathcal{X}^{i}
such that
q_{i}\in\Sigma_{i}
.

Define the time intervals
\mathbb{I}^{++}=[-10,0]
,
\mathbb{I}^{+}=[-9.9,0]
,
\mathbb{I}=[-9.8,0]
and
\mathbb{I}^{-}=(-9.8,0]
. Then, by Theorem
2.17
, passing to a subsequence if necessary, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{i},d_{i}^{*},q_{i}^{*},\mathfrak{t}_{i}\right)\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{\hskip 8.19447pt\mathrm{pGH}\hskip 8.19447pt}\left(Z,d_{Z},q,\mathfrak{t}\right),

(6.35)

where
d_{i}^{*}
denotes the spacetime distance induced by
g_{i}(t)
(see Definition
2.16
). In particular,
\left(Z,d_{Z},q,\mathfrak{t}\right)
is a noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space over
\mathbb{I}
. In addition, the convergence (
6.35
) is smooth on the regular part
\mathcal{R}
of
Z
in the sense of Theorem
2.19
. Note that
\mathcal{R}
carries a structure of Ricci flow spacetime
(\mathcal{R},\mathfrak{t},\partial_{\mathfrak{t}},g^{Z})
. Let
\phi_{i}
denote the diffeomorphisms given in Theorem
2.19
.

It follows from Proposition
6.2
that there exists a constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(n)>0
such that

\displaystyle r^{i}_{\mathrm{Rm}}(x^{*})\geq c_{0}

for any
