which establishes the desired lower bound in (
8.10
).

Since
L_{i}+20D_{1}>\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|,i}+D_{0}
, combining (
8.9
), (
8.10
) and using the fact that
\varphi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
is an extension of
\varphi_{A,i}
, we obtain a contradiction for sufficiently large
i
.
∎

Next, we define

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{l}=\bar{D}_{l}(n,\sigma)=10^{4}\bar{\delta}_{l}^{-1}\gg 1.

(8.12)

By Theorem
8.6
, we fix a smooth covering map
\tilde{\varphi}_{A}
of degree
|\Gamma|
from a domain
U
containing
\{\bar{b}\leq\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}+\bar{D}_{l}\}
onto a subset of
M
such that

\displaystyle\left[\tilde{\varphi}_{A}^{*}g(-1)-\bar{g}\right]_{2}\leq\bar{\delta}_{l},

on
U
, and for any
x
with
\bar{f}(x)\geq 10n+\log|\Gamma|
, it holds

\displaystyle(1-\bar{\delta}_{l})\left(\bar{f}(x)-\log|\Gamma|\right)\leq f(-1)\circ\tilde{\varphi}_{A}^{*}(x)\leq(1+\bar{\delta}_{l})\left(\bar{f}(x)-\log|\Gamma|\right).

And on
\{\bar{b}\leq\mathbf{r}^{|\Gamma|}_{A}-3\bar{\delta}_{l}^{-1}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\bar{g}-\tilde{\varphi}_{A}^{*}g(-1)\right]_{5}+\left[\bar{f}-\log|\Gamma|-\tilde{\varphi}_{A}^{*}f(-1)\right]_{5}\leq e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{4}-\frac{\textbf{r}_{A}^{2}}{16}}.

Next, we extend Theorem
6.18
; see also
[
LZ23
, Theorem 5.7]
.

Theorem 8.7
.

There exists a large constant
\hat{L}=\hat{L}(n,Y,\sigma,|\Gamma|)>1
such that if
\mathbf{r}^{|\Gamma|}_{A}\in[\hat{L},(1-2\sigma)\mathbf{r}_{E}]
, then

\displaystyle\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}\geq\mathbf{r}^{|\Gamma|}_{A}+\bar{D}_{100}/10.

Here, both
\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}
and
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}
denote the radius functions for
(M,g(-1),f(-1))
(cf. Definition
8.1
), and
\bar{D}_{100}
is the constant defined in (
8.12
) with
l=100
.

Proof.

We set
L=\mathbf{r}^{|\Gamma|}_{A}-3\bar{\delta}_{100}^{-1}
and
\bar{D}=\bar{D}_{100}
. Applying the same proof of Theorem
6.18
to

\displaystyle(g_{0},f_{0}):=\left(\tilde{\varphi}_{A}^{*}g(-1),\tilde{\varphi}_{A}^{*}f(-1)+\log|\Gamma|\right),

we obtain a diffeomorphism
\psi_{5}
from
B_{g_{E}}(0,L+0.3\bar{D})\times S^{m}
into
\bar{M}
(see (
6.70
), (
6.71
), (
6.72
) and (
6.73
)) such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\psi_{5}^{*}g_{0}-\bar{g}\right\rvert+\left\lvert\psi_{5}^{*}f_{0}-\bar{f}\right\rvert\leq C\exp\left(CL^{\frac{3}{2}}-\frac{L^{2}}{32}\right)

and for any
k\in[1,10^{11}n\sigma^{-1}]
,

\displaystyle\|\psi_{5}^{*}g_{0}-\bar{g}\|_{C^{k}}+\|\psi_{5}^{*}f_{0}-\bar{f}\|_{C^{k}}\leq e^{-\frac{L^{2}}{33}}.

We consider
\Omega:=\{b\leq L+0.2\bar{D}\}\cap\tilde{\varphi}_{A}\left(U\right)
, where
b:=2\sqrt{|f(-1)|}
. Then it is clear that
U^{\prime}:=\{\tilde{\varphi}_{A}^{-1}\left(\Omega\right)\}
satisfies

\displaystyle\{\bar{b}\leq L+0.1\bar{D}\}\subset U^{\prime}\subset\{\bar{b}\leq L+0.3\bar{D}\}.

Consequently, if we define
\varphi_{B}:=\tilde{\varphi}_{A}\circ\psi_{5}
from
\psi_{5}^{-1}(U^{\prime})
, then it is clear that
\varphi_{B}
satisfies the conclusions for
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}
in Definition
8.1
, and hence we have

\displaystyle\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}\geq\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}+0.1\bar{D}.

In sum, the proof is complete. ∎

The following result is parallel to Theorem
6.24
by using Proposition
8.2
and Theorem
8.7
iteratively.

Theorem 8.8
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M^{n},(g(t))_{t\in I}\}
be a closed Ricci flow with entropy bounded below by
-Y
. Assume
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},0)\in\mathcal{X}
and
[-10,0]\subset I
. For any small
\sigma\in(0,1/10)
, there exists a constant
L=L(n,Y,\sigma,|\Gamma|)
such that if the weighted Riemannian manifold
\left(M,g(-1),f_{x_{0}^{*}}(-1)\right)
satisfies
\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}\geq L
, then

\displaystyle\min\left\{\mathbf{r}^{|\Gamma|}_{A},\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}\right\}\geq(1-3\sigma)\mathbf{r}_{E}.

From the above results, we obtain the following Lojasiewicz inequality near quotient cylinders, whose proof is almost identical to that of Theorem
6.26
.

Theorem 8.9

(Lojasiewicz inequality)
.

For any
\beta\in(0,3/4)
, there exist constants
C=C(n,Y)
and
L_{\beta}=L(n,Y,\beta,|\Gamma|)
such that the following property holds.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M,g(t)\}_{t\in I}
be a closed Ricci flow with entropy bounded below by
-Y
. Assume
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}
and
[t_{0}-10r^{2},t_{0}]\subset I
. If the weighted Riemannian manifold
\left(M,r^{-2}g(t_{0}-r^{2}),f_{x_{0}^{*}}(t_{0}-r^{2})\right)
satisfies
\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}\geq L_{\beta}
, then

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r^{2})-\Theta_{m}(\Gamma)\right|\leq C\left(\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r^{2}/2)-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(2r^{2})\right)^{\beta}.

We remark that Theorems
6.25
and
6.27
also hold in the quotient case and leave the precise statements to the interested readers.

Strong uniqueness for quotient cylindrical singularities

Let
\mathcal{C}^{k}(\Gamma)_{-1}
be a quotient cylinder as in (
8.1
). We define
\mathcal{C}^{k}(\Gamma):=(\bar{M},(\bar{g}_{\Gamma}(t))_{t<0},(\bar{f}_{\Gamma}(t))_{t<0})
to be the associated Ricci flow, where
t=0
corresponds to the singular time, and the potential function is given by

\displaystyle\pi^{*}\bar{f}_{\Gamma}(t)=\frac{|\vec{x}|^{2}}{4|t|}+\frac{n-k}{4}+\Theta_{n-k}-\log|\Gamma|,

where
\pi
is the quotient map from
\mathcal{C}^{k}
to
\mathcal{C}^{k}(\Gamma)
.

Let
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
be the completion of
\mathcal{C}^{k}(\Gamma)
, equipped with the spacetime distance
d_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
. We also define the base point
p^{*}
as the limit of
(\bar{p},t)
as
t\nearrow 0
with respect to
d_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}
, where
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{C}^{k}(\Gamma)
is a minimum point of
\bar{f}_{\Gamma}(-1)
. For any
t<0
, we have

\displaystyle\nu_{p^{*};t}=(4\pi|t|)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\bar{f}_{\Gamma}(t)}\,\mathrm{d}V_{\bar{g}_{\Gamma}(t)}.

Next, we consider a general noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
over
\mathbb{I}
, obtained as the limit of a sequence in
\mathcal{M}(n,T,Y)
. Then, we have the following definition similar to Definition
2.22
.

Definition 8.10
.

A point
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
is called
a quotient cylindrical point with respect to
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
if a tangent flow at
z
is
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
for some
k
and
\Gamma
.

The following theorem regarding the uniqueness of the quotient cylindrical tangent flow can be proved as Theorem
2.23
by using
[
LZ23
, Theorem 6.2, Remark 6.3]
.

Theorem 8.11

(Uniqueness of the quotient cylindrical tangent flow)
.

Let
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
be a noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space over
\mathbb{I}
, obtained as the limit of a sequence in
\mathcal{M}(n,T,Y)
. For any
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
, if a tangent flow at
z
is
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
, then any tangent flow at
z
is also
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
.

Next, we define almost quotient cylindrical points similar to Definition
2.26
.

Definition 8.12
.

Let
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
be a noncollapsed Ricci flow limit space over
\mathbb{I}
, obtained as the limit of a sequence in
\mathcal{M}(n,T,Y)
. A point
z\in Z_{\mathbb{I}^{-}}
is called
(\epsilon,r)
-close to
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
if
\mathfrak{t}(z)-\epsilon^{-1}r^{2}\in\mathbb{I}^{-}
and

\displaystyle\big(Z,r^{-1}d_{Z},z,r^{-2}(\mathfrak{t}-\mathfrak{t}(z))\big)\quad\text{is $\epsilon$-close to}\quad\big(\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma),d_{\mathcal{C}}^{*},p^{*},\mathfrak{t}\big)\quad\text{over}\quad[-\epsilon^{-1},\epsilon^{-1}].

By the identical proofs, we obtain the following results parallel to Corollary
6.28
, Theorems
7.1
and
7.6
, respectively.

Corollary 8.13
.

For any constants
0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon(n,Y)
,
\alpha\in(1/4,1)
and
\delta\leq\delta(n,Y,\epsilon,\alpha)
, the following holds. Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M^{n},(g(t))_{t\in I}\}
be a closed Ricci flow with entropy bounded below by
-Y
. Fix
x_{0}^{*}=(x_{0},t_{0})\in\mathcal{X}
and constants
s_{2}>s_{1}>0
. If

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(s_{1})-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(s_{2})\right|<\delta,

and, for any
s\in[s_{1},s_{2}]
,
x_{0}^{*}
is
(\delta,\sqrt{s})
-close to
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{1}\leq r_{j}=2^{-j}\leq s_{2}}\left|\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r_{j})-\mathcal{W}_{x_{0}^{*}}(r_{j-1})\right|^{\alpha}<\epsilon.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M^{n},(g(t))_{t\in[-T,0)}\}
be a closed Ricci flow with entropy bounded below by
-Y
, where
0
is the first singular time. Suppose
(Z,d_{Z},\mathfrak{t})
is the completion of
\mathcal{X}
. Fix a point
z\in Z_{0}
and consider the modified Ricci flow
(M,g^{z}(s),f^{z}(s))
with respect to
z
. We denote by
\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}(s)
and
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}(s)
the radius functions as in Definition
8.1
with respect to
(M,g^{z}(s),f^{z}(s))
.

Theorem 8.14
.

Suppose
z
is a quotient cylindrical singularity with respect to
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
. Then for any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\min\left\{\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}(s),\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}(s)\right\}\geq\sqrt{(12-\epsilon)\log s},

where
\sigma=10^{-3}\epsilon
, provided that
s
is sufficiently large.

Next, we prove the strong uniqueness of the quotient cylindrical tangent flow. For simplicity, we set
\bar{b}_{\Gamma}:=2\sqrt{|\bar{f}_{\Gamma}|}
on
\mathcal{C}^{k}(\Gamma)
.

Theorem 8.15

(Strong uniqueness of the quotient cylindrical tangent flow)
.

Suppose
z
is a quotient cylindrical singularity with respect to
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
. Then for any small
\epsilon>0
, there exists a large constant
\bar{s}
such that for any
j\geq\bar{s}
, there exists a diffeomorphism from
\Omega^{j}:=\left\{\bar{b}_{\Gamma}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)\log j}\right\}\subset\bar{M}/\Gamma
onto a subset of
M
such that

\displaystyle\psi_{j+1}=\psi_{j}\quad\mathrm{on}\quad\Omega^{j},

and for all
s\geq j
, we have on
\Omega^{j}
,

\displaystyle\left[\psi_{j}^{*}g^{z}(s)-\bar{g}_{\Gamma}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}+\left[\psi_{j}^{*}f^{z}(s)-\bar{f}_{\Gamma}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{\frac{\bar{f}_{\Gamma}}{2}}s^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Here, the
C^{[\epsilon^{-1}]}
-norms are with respect to
\bar{g}_{\Gamma}
.

Theorem
8.15
follows, by the same argument as in Theorem
7.6
, immediately from the next result.

Theorem 8.16
.

Suppose
z
is a quotient cylindrical singularity with respect to
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
. Then for any small
\epsilon>0
, there exists a large constant
\bar{s}
such that for any
s_{0}\geq\bar{s}
, there exists a diffeomorphism
\varphi^{\Gamma}_{s_{0}}
from
\left\{\bar{b}_{\Gamma}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)\log s_{0}}\right\}
onto a subset of
M
such that on
\left\{\bar{b}_{\Gamma}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)\log s_{0}}\right\}
and for all
s\geq s_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left[(\varphi^{\Gamma}_{s_{0}})^{*}g^{z}(s)-\bar{g}_{\Gamma}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}+\left[(\varphi^{\Gamma}_{s_{0}})^{*}f^{z}(s)-\bar{f}_{\Gamma}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{\frac{\bar{f}_{\Gamma}}{2}}s^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Here, the
C^{[\epsilon^{-1}]}
-norms are with respect to
\bar{g}_{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

Because the proof is similar to that of Theorem
7.2
, we sketch the argument and detail only the new ingredients.

Throughout the proof, fix a small constant
\epsilon>0
and set

\displaystyle\Omega_{s}:=\varphi_{s}\left(\left\{\bar{b}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/3)\log s}\right\}\right)\subset M,

where
\varphi_{s}
denotes the map, restricted to
\left\{\bar{b}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/3)\log s}\right\}
, corresponding to
\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}(s)
in Definition
8.1
.

From Definition
8.1
and Theorem
8.14
, for all sufficiently large
s
,

\displaystyle\left[\bar{g}-\varphi_{s}^{*}g^{z}(s)\right]_{5}+\left[\bar{f}-\log|\Gamma|-\varphi_{s}^{*}f^{z}(s)\right]_{5}\leq e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{4}}s^{-\frac{3}{4}+\epsilon}.

(8.13)

Let
\bar{p}
be a minimum point of
\bar{f}
, and set
p_{s}=\varphi_{s}(\bar{p})
. Then, by (
8.13
), we have
f^{z}(s)(p_{s})\leq C(n,Y)
for all large
s
.

Claim 8.17
.

For all sufficiently large
s
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{x\mid d_{g^{z}(s)}(x,p_{s})\leq 2\sqrt{(1-0.6\epsilon)\log s}\right\}\subset\varphi_{s}\left(\left\{\bar{b}<2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s}\right\}\right).

Fix
x
with
d_{g^{z}(s)}(x,p_{s})\leq 2\sqrt{(1-0.6\epsilon)\log s}
, and let
\gamma(z):[0,L]\to M
be a minimizing geodesic with respect to
g^{z}(s)
from
p_{s}
to
x
, where
L=d_{g^{z}(s)}(x,p_{s})
. Suppose, toward a contradiction, that

\displaystyle x\notin\varphi_{s}\left(\left\{\bar{b}<2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s}\right\}\right).

Define
z_{0}
to be the largest number
z\in[0,L]
so that
\gamma
, when restricted to
[0,z]
, is contained in
\varphi_{s}\left(\left\{\bar{b}<2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s}\right\}\right)
. Since
\varphi_{s}
is a local diffeomorphism on
\left\{\bar{b}<2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s}\right\}
, we can lift
\gamma|_{[0,z_{0})}
to a curve
\tilde{\gamma}
in
\left\{\bar{b}<2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s}\right\}
starting from
\bar{p}
. By our assumption and (
8.13
), the length of
\tilde{\gamma}
, with respect to
\bar{g}
, is at most
2\sqrt{(1-0.55\epsilon)\log s}
, for sufficiently large
s
. However, this implies, by (
8.13
) again, that
\tilde{\gamma}
is contained in
\left\{\bar{b}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-0.51\epsilon)\log s}\right\}
. This contradicts the definition of
z_{0}
, proving Claim
8.17
.

As in the proof of Theorem
7.2
, Theorem
8.14
yields, on
\Omega_{s}
,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{g^{z}(s)}{2}-\nabla^{2}f^{z}(s)-\mathrm{Ric}(g^{z}(s))\right]_{l}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon,l)s^{-1-\frac{\epsilon}{24}},

(8.14)

and

\displaystyle\left[\frac{n}{2}-\Delta f^{z}(s)-\mathrm{R}(g^{z}(s))\right]_{l}\leq C_{1}(n,Y,\epsilon,l)s^{-1-\frac{\epsilon}{24}}

(8.15)

for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, provided that
s
is sufficiently large.

Choose
\bar{s}
large so that (
8.14
), (
8.15
) and Claim
8.17
hold with
l=[\epsilon^{-1}]
for all
s\geq\bar{s}
. Moreover, as in Claim
7.3
, we may assume that for a fixed
s_{0}\geq\bar{s}
,

\displaystyle\Omega^{s_{0}}:=\varphi_{s_{0}}\left(\left\{\bar{b}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s_{0}}\right\}\right)\subset\Omega_{s},\quad\forall s\geq s_{0}.

Thus,
\left(\varphi_{s_{0}}^{*}g^{z}(s),\varphi_{s_{0}}^{*}f^{z}(s)\right)
converges smoothly to
\left(g_{\infty},f_{\infty}\right)
on
\left\{\bar{b}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s_{0}}\right\}
as
s\to\infty
. In addition, for all
s\geq s_{0}
,

\displaystyle\left[\varphi_{s_{0}}^{*}g^{z}(s)-g_{\infty}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}+\left[\varphi_{s_{0}}^{*}f^{z}(s)-f_{\infty}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{\frac{\bar{f}}{2}}s^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{8}}.

(8.16)

Take a sequence
s_{i}\to\infty
, then by our assumption,

\displaystyle\left(M,g^{z}(s_{i}),f^{z}(s_{i}),p_{s_{i}}\right)\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{\hskip 8.19447pt\text{pointed-Cheeger--Gromov}\hskip 8.19447pt}\left(\bar{M}/\Gamma,\bar{g}_{\Gamma},\bar{f}_{\Gamma},\bar{p}_{\Gamma}\right),

where
\bar{p}_{\Gamma}
is a minimum point of
\bar{f}_{\Gamma}
. Thus, for sufficiently large
i
, there exists a diffeomorphism
\psi_{i}:U_{i}\subset\bar{M}/\Gamma\to M
, such that
U_{i}\subset U_{i+1}
, and
\bigcup_{i}U_{i}=\bar{M}/\Gamma
, satisfying

\displaystyle\psi_{i}(\bar{p}_{\Gamma})=p_{s_{i}},\quad\psi_{i}^{*}g^{z}(s_{i})\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}}\bar{g}_{\Gamma},\quad\psi_{i}^{*}f^{z}(s_{i})\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\infty}}\bar{f}_{\Gamma}.

(8.17)

Next, we set
V_{i}:=\psi_{i}^{-1}\left(\Omega^{s_{0}}\right)\subset U_{i}
, which is well-defined for large
i
. Combining Claim
8.17
with (
8.17
), we obtain, for all sufficiently large
i
,

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}:=\left\{\bar{b}_{\Gamma}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)\log s_{0}}\right\}\subset V_{i}.

(8.18)

Since
\left(\varphi_{s_{0}}^{*}g^{z}(s),\varphi_{s_{0}}^{*}f^{z}(s)\right)
converge smoothly to
\left(g_{\infty},f_{\infty}\right)
on
\left\{\bar{b}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon/2)\log s_{0}}\right\}
, it follows from (
8.17
) and (
8.18
) that
\psi_{i}:\Omega^{\prime}\to\Omega^{s_{0}}
converge smoothly to a diffeomorphism
\varphi^{\Gamma}_{s_{0}}:\Omega^{\prime}\to\Omega^{s_{0}}
such that

\displaystyle\left((\varphi^{\Gamma}_{s_{0}})^{-1}\circ\varphi_{s_{0}}\right)^{*}\bar{g}_{\Gamma}=g_{\infty}\quad\text{and}\quad\left((\varphi^{\Gamma}_{s_{0}})^{-1}\circ\varphi_{s_{0}}\right)^{*}\bar{f}_{\Gamma}=f_{\infty}.

Consequently, the desired estimate follows from (
8.16
) (after adjusting constant
\epsilon
), completing the proof.
∎

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{M^{n},(g(t))_{t\in(-\infty,0]}\}
be a complete Ricci flow with bounded curvature on any compact time interval of
(-\infty,0]
and with entropy bounded below. For a fixed base point
p^{*}\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
and set
\mathrm{d}\nu_{p^{*};t}=(4\pi|t|)^{-n/2}e^{-f(t)}\,\mathrm{d}V_{g(t)}
. Let
\phi_{t}
be the family of diffeomorphisms generated by
-\nabla_{g(t)}f(t)
with
\phi_{0}=\mathrm{id}
. As (
7.12
), we consider

\begin{cases}&g^{\prime}(s):=e^{s}\phi^{*}_{-e^{-s}}g(-e^{-s}),\\
&f^{\prime}(s):=\phi^{*}_{-e^{-s}}f_{z}(-e^{-s}).\\
\end{cases}

The following results are parallel to Theorems
7.9
and
7.10
.

Theorem 8.18
.

Suppose
\mathcal{X}
admits
\bar{\mathcal{C}}^{k}(\Gamma)
as a tangent flow at infinity. Let
\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}(s)
and
\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}(s)
be the radius functions from Definition
8.1
with respect to
(M,g^{\prime}(s),f^{\prime}(s))
. Then,

\displaystyle\min\left\{\textbf{r}_{A}^{|\Gamma|}(s),\textbf{r}_{B,\sigma}^{|\Gamma|}(s)\right\}\geq\sqrt{(12-\epsilon)\log|s|},

where
\sigma=10^{-3}\epsilon
, provided that
|s|
is sufficiently large.

Theorem 8.19

(Strong uniqueness of quotient cylindrical tangent flow at infinity)
.

Under the same assumptions of Theorem
8.18
, for any small
\epsilon>0
, there exists a diffeomorphism from
\Omega^{j}:=\left\{\bar{b}_{\Gamma}\leq 2\sqrt{(1-\epsilon)\log j}\right\}\subset\bar{M}/\Gamma
onto a subset of
M
such that

\displaystyle\psi_{-j-1}=\psi_{-j}\quad\mathrm{on}\quad\Omega^{j},

and for any
s\leq-j
, we have on
\Omega^{j}
,

\displaystyle\left[\psi_{-j}^{*}g^{\prime}(s)-\bar{g}_{\Gamma}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}+\left[\psi_{-j}^{*}f^{\prime}(s)-\bar{f}_{\Gamma}\right]_{[\epsilon^{-1}]}\leq C(n,Y,\epsilon)e^{\frac{\bar{f}_{\Gamma}}{2}}|s|^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.
